4548. A palm-sized stone, flat and ellipsoidal, made from roughly hewn marble. In the centre is a slight indentation, polished mirror smooth from countless thumbs rubbing circles over the years. Some creatures who uses the worry stone claim that catching their own reflection in this divot brings a sense of serenity and peace of mind.
4549. A pamphlet announcing upcoming show by Brusker & Piemoore, a controversial comedy duo that consists of a fat elf and a bald dwarf.
4550. A pamphlet for a lecture on the differences between gnomes and halflings in a town not too far away.
4551. A pamphlet for a new church in an unfamiliar town. It details their strong beliefs in polyamorous relationships and their condemnation for magic of any kind.
4552. A pamphlet listing fourteen magical fountains and their effects when touched. It does not list their locations, directions to them or what guards them.
4553. A pan flute fashioned from hollowed out oak twigs.
4554. A paper bird that briefly flaps its wings and flies when thrown
4555. A paper fan that won’t produce a breeze no matter how hard it’s waved.
4556. A paper with an illustration of a beautiful woman with ink hearts around it and the name of a city. On the back is a love poem dedicated to the woman. A well read PC will recognize the woman as a purely fictional character from a romance novel.
4557. A papyrus scroll depicting a ritual disembowelling.
4558. A parchment showing the constellations in the night sky.
4559. A parrot-sized urn of ashes with the name “Petey”.
4560. A particularly shiny red rock that constantly remains hot enough to be unpleasant to touch but never actually harmful.
4561. A pauldron worn on the right shoulder made of white stained leather. A symbol on it denotes a priest’s position in the church’s hierarchy.
4562. A peacock-feather quill that always writes smoothly and never needs to be filled with ink.
4563. A pearl necklace, when there is an eclipse the pearls change from white to a black colour.
4564. A pearlescent marble scepter topped by a blue gemstone cut in the shape of an eight-pointed star.
4565. A pebble, delicately carved to resemble an infamous dwarven mine baron.
4566. A pecan wand with a floating bauble made of peridot at the tip
4567. A peg leg inscribed with a pirate’s treasure map. Knowledgeable PC’s will notice slight irregularities in the etched map and can determine that following it would not lead to anything prosperous. Perceptive PC’s will notice a small hidden compartment within the leg that contains the true treasure map.
4568. A pegboard with ten pegs. The pegs change color based on geometric patterns and proximity to the other pegs
4569. A pen of exquisite craftsmanship with a body like smooth, polished marble and gold trimming. The pen has a small golden leaver where in the tip of the pen can be touched to the shadow of an individual’s head and enough ink for a full sheet of paper is extracted.
4570. A pencil-on-paper schematic of a crossbow-like contraption of tubes, triggers and optics.
4571. A penny whistle that plays the same note no matter which holes are covered
4572. A perfect forgery of a masterpiece work of art. It’s a fraction of the size of the original and could never pass as authentic.
4573. A perfect specimen of a pine cone, inexplicably encased in a rectangular block of clear glass.
4574. A perfect specimen of an acorn encased in a small cube of clear glass.
4575. A perfectly fresh pineapple that has somehow resisted the ravages of time.
4576. A perfectly preserved human brain, encased in a large dome of clear glass.
4577. A perfectly preserved human heart, encased in a clear glass cube.
4578. A perfectly preserved hummingbird, its wings fully spread as if in flight, encased in a clear glass cube.
4579. A perfectly preserved locust encased in a small glass cube. It’s wings are fully spread and extended as if it was flying. A creature holding the dead insect is filled with a deep, primal hunger that can never be satisfied so long as the locust is carried.
4580. A perfectly preserved rabbit skull, encased in a glass cube.
4581. A perfectly preserved sky lily that knowledgeable PC’s will recognize as the only species that grows at the top of the God’s Head, an incredibly high mountain.
4582. A perfectly preserved tarantula, encased in a glass hemisphere.
4583. A perfectly preserved, brightly coloured dragonfly, encased in a block of clear glass.
4584. A perfectly preserved, iridescent emerald green, praying mantis, encased in a small glass prism. It’s reared back on it’s hind legs and it’s claws are extended, eternally frozen mid strike.
4585. A perfectly round ball of translucent crystal as black as the night sky. Floating in the darkness of the ball are twinkling motes of white light, and seven steady coloured lights. Looking into this darkened orb is like looking out a window onto the night sky.
4586. A perfectly round black obsidian orb two inches in diameter.
4587. A perfectly smooth clear glass square, roughly arm-length along each side, with bevelled edges and rounded corners. It may have once been a table top.
4588. A perfectly smooth stone tablet covered in faintly glowing symbols that move quietly over it’s surface. The symbols don’t seem to resemble any known language or code.
4589. A perfectly smooth stone which, when squeezed, turns blue for five minutes.
4590. A performers trick blade that looks and feels like a normal dagger, but when thrust into something the blade retracts into an empty compartment in the hilt and causes no damage.
4591. A perfume vinaigrette shaped like a tiny, long amphora. Made of some silvery metal and worked all over with tarnished curlicue. If shaken, the vinaigrette rattles, as if filled with large grains. Its lid clicks open, allowing, from the grated neck, a mossy odor of chypre. A scent neither in vogue nor disliked, today. The scent does not run out, nor fade.
4592. A perpetually wet whetstone.
4593. A perverse and gaudy replica of a cleric’s mask. When this facial covering is worn, the spirit feels nebulous, boundless even. The vast distances between each being dissolves, revealing a vision of the world beheld by a thousandfold eyes.
4594. A petrified basilisk’s egg carved into an elaborate diorama of a strange but beautiful landscape.
4595. A petrified cockatrice egg
4596. A petrified egg, slightly orange, with one side cut away. A hideous embryo, also fossilized, is curled there. It is a long-backed, anencephalic neonate with long, rodent-like incisors that join to form a sort of beak. It clutches, in three-fingered hands, its own tail. The thing is shot through with long maggots turned by time into red stone.
4597. A petrified frog
4598. A petrified goblin heart in a silver birdcage.
4599. A petrified human heart that regularly leaks an oily black substance
4600. A petrified mouse
4601. A petrified pixie that would make a cute paperweight.
4602. A petrified potato that resembles someone to whoever looks at it
4603. A petrified toad with a variety of crystals growing from its back, diverse in material, color, size, and shape.
4604. A pewter acorn with a lead cap and stem. Something sloshes heavily inside, like mercury. The cap does not open.
4605. A pewter brooch with a galloping horse embossed on it.
4606. A pewter goblet with dark and rancid blood lurking within. The lip is black and caked where it appears others have tasted from it. Scratched deep into the pewter are the words “Taste My Fear.”
4607. A pickled and dried human hand of a murderer, tightly clutching a tallow candle made from his fat, made with one of the murderer’s hairs for a wick.
4608. A picture amulet showing an opalescent blue altar.
4609. A piece of amber containing an insect wearing a tiny saddle and halter.
4610. A piece of animal hide covered in strange white rune, when stored occasionally quiet laughter is heard from the hide.
4611. A piece of black stained parchment that pertains to be a map of the Underdark.
4612. A piece of black stone with a strange trilobite creature etched onto it.
4613. A piece of clear quartz that turns a light pink colour whenever blood is shed within sight of it.
4614. A piece of cloth written with a short prophecy.
4615. A piece of cobblestone rumoured to have once made up the road that lead to a famed lost city from the dawn of civilization.
4616. A piece of crimson coral carved into the shape of a shark.
4617. A piece of crystal that faintly glows in the moonlight
4618. A piece of dark green stone with golden flecks in it. Anyone who sleeps near the stone has unsettling dreams of wandering through a snow covered city of impossible angles.
4619. A piece of exotic wood, carved to look like a quill, with a gold tip. Its purpose is obviously ceremonial and no doubt used for signing important decrees or documents, as it is awkward to write with.
4620. A piece of green coral carved into the shape of a turtle.
4621. A piece of paper that refuses to become uncrumpled until a spell similar to Dispel Magic or Remove Curse is cast on it. Inside is the true name of a weak outsider such as an angel or demon.
4622. A piece of parchment covered with untranslatable demonic script.
4623. A piece of parchment torn from a notebook, written on it are a list of names and causes of death.
4624. A piece of parchment with phrase ‘The spectre of Samantha Tholun still roams her ancestral halls’ written on it.
4625. A piece of rock that changes color depending on the current weather.
4626. A piece of sandstone carved into a monkey fist knot the size of an apple.
4627. A piece of stone on which someone has expertly engraved a portrait of a young man.
4628. A piece of stone that changes colour based on the mood of it’s owner.
4629. A piece of string that randomly emits smoke.
4630. A piece of strong paper full of written symbols. Every few days, one of the symbols rises off the paper, becoming three dimensional, and disengages from the paper. A new symbol appears on the page to take its place.
4631. A piece of torn red cloth bearing a royal insignia.
4632. A piece of volcanic rock that has never completely cooled down and is always warm to the touch.
4633. A piece of waterproof paper that can’t be crumpled and shows a map of somewhere unknown
4634. A piece of weathered parchment upon which words occasionally appear and disappear at random
4635. A piece of white cloth. When it is laid over the face of a dead person it holds their likeness for twenty four hours before returning to its blank state.
4636. A pillow made out of supple waterproof leather and stuffed with duck down. It is strong enough to be used as an adventurer’s travel pillow
4637. A pine cone that resembles a bearded man’s face
4638. A pink apron with “Seasoned adventurers have better taste!” written on it.
4639. A pink stone sculpture of an ear which grows warm when it hears false flattery.
4640. A pipe made from the leg bone of an animal. When played it creates a series of high-pitched shrieks.
4641. A pipe that blows bubbles
4642. A pirate flag that although ragged around the edges, clearly shows a grinning skull with devil horns, and crossed bastard swords. Knowledgeable PC’s recognize this flag belongs to Garrin Firebrand, the Reef Lord. A local pirate of some distinction, his ship always seems to vanish as soon as it hits open water, which has caused many to wonder if Firebrand and his crew have found a way to slip beneath the waves.
4643. A pitted fist of ore, quite heavy, that contains strange fossils. Neither the bony, many-legged fossils nor the metal within the ore are readily identifiable as any known to alchemical sciences or arcane arts.
4644. A plaid kilt with a tag in the back that states the owner is “The Greatest Adventurer Who Ever Lived.”
4645. A plain copper bracelet. Several small charms (demon heads, claws and other fell things) hang from the bracelet and quietly jangle when moved. The charms are well made, but obviously evil in nature.
4646. A plain dark wooden rod of elven design. The maker’s mark suggests that it used to belong to royalty
4647. A plain oak box filled with gnome sized plumbing tools.
4648. A plain oaken case, the inside of which is lined in plush, royal blue velvet. The velvet has three indents on which lie three ordinary looking pine cones.
4649. A plain scroll tube containing several pieces of parchment that were used to make observations of the stars. These comprise several complicated diagrams of various constellations and cryptic notes regarding “the wanderer.”
4650. A plain-looking wooden bowl of water, which remains unnaturally still while inside it. Looking at one’s reflection in the water shows an idealized version of oneself, in accordance to the viewer’s ideals. Perhaps by lowering one’s head into the bowl for long enough, one can gain insight into how to become the self seen in the bowl.
4651. A plainsman’s woven wolf idol.
4652. A plate of glass that turns frosty in warm temperatures and sometimes shows hand prints in the frost
4653. A plate of glass that, when used to view the night sky through it, reveals ten times as many stars
4654. A playing card showing the face of a grinning halfling child. When a creature rubs their thumb over the card, it changes the face on the card to a different one. The change is random and can be any myriad of races, ages, sexes, hair and skin colors as well as facial expressions. There doesn’t seem to be a trick to getting a specific face to appear.
4655. A plush doll of a male human wearing hunting clothing, a crossbow and a sword. There’s a slit on the doll’s back allowing it to be turned inside out, turning him into a black dire wolf. Both only have one eye.
4656. A plush velvet bag filled with small twigs from random trees.
4657. A pocket mirror that reflects men as women and vice-versa.
4658. A pocket sized hand-bellows that were probably meant as a child’s toy but are fully functional and useful for starting fires.
4659. A pocket watch that runs backward for an hour every midnight
4660. A pocket watch with a strange design of interlinked circles on it. Sometimes it whispers to its bearer about time and space.
4661. A pocket-size magnet that attracts wood instead of metal.
4662. A pocket-watch that starts running backwards whenever it is held
4663. A polished copper wristband that smells strongly of sugar
4664. A polished dark wood box inlaid with silver tracery and lined with velvet, suitable for displaying a single piece of jewellery.
4665. A polished jade hair band studded with tiny black stones which sparkle with a faint, internal light.
4666. A polished marble model of a human heart, nearly the size of a human heart. Although it looks like a single solid piece, it weighs next to nothing.
4667. A polished metal case containing a set of fine needles, similar to those for sewing, several thin glass cylinders, and a single ampoule of a sluggish red fluid.
4668. A polished mirror in a simple wooden frame. Looking into it for too long makes people feel uneasy in a way they cannot fully describe.
4669. A polished shard of translucent black crystal.
4670. A polished steel badge bearing the insignia of the local city watch
4671. A polished stone bearing a mystic sign that resembles a lopsided, two-barred cross.
4672. A polished wooden carving of a fish that turns a vibrant green when placed in water.
4673. A poorly cultivated bonsai juniper in a glazed ceramic pot
4674. A poorly made porcelain vase with gold leaf randomly placed on it.
4675. A porcelain doll that looks like a troll.
4676. A porcelain doll’s head that always seems to be looking at the nearest creature to it
4677. A porcelain kami doll that has been painted all black.
4678. A porcelain mask bearing cracks across it. The bottom right of the face from the jawline to the cheekbone to the chin is broken off. A viewer can just make out the expression of terror carved into the remaining features of the mask.
4679. A porcelain mask depicting a slightly disgusted visage of an oligarch.
4680. A porcelain mask featuring a beautiful womanly face with closed eyes. The porcelain has a slight golden hue and gleam to it.
4681. A porcelain mask featuring a laughing face and a wide open mouth, and has all manner of colours and inlays on it. Inside the open mouth is just a black void that reflects no light, not even a glossy sheen.
4682. A porcelain mask resembles a skull with its mouth sowed shut.
4683. A porcelain mortar and pestle decorated with a painted pattern of dancing faeries.
4684. A porcelain pitcher with arboreal imagery.
4685. A porcelain teapot inscribed with ancient symbols. A blue snake-like dragon coils around the pot, its body forming the handle and its mouth forming the spout.
4686. A porous stone flecked with emerald and sapphire dust that always feels damp to the touch.
4687. A portable battering ram consisting of a hollow steel tube with a steel pad at the impact end which absorbs the blow, a handle at the opposite end angled so that the wielder can swing accurately without actually applying his own pressure more than necessary and a handle in the middle of the tube aiding the user in handling. A creature using the ram gains advantage on strength checks made to break down doors and other objects.
4688. A portable battering ram consisting of iron-bound oak with the head of a snarling bear as its battering end. The bear’s heavy iron head has stunningly blue onyx eyes.
4689. A portable battering ram with the words “Master Key” written in white paint
4690. A portable bronze planar armillary sphere whose dials rotate to show the relation of the inner and outer planes to the prime material, as well as the influence of the Gods and planets, at any time during the year.
4691. A portable iron anvil that weighs ten pounds. The anvil can be used for wire and jewelry work as well, as creating small objects or assisting with light repair work.
4692. A portable stretcher made of two study wooden shafts and a thick sheet of canvas. It allows two people to share the burden of carrying one heavy object. It can also be used to drag a load a creature couldn’t carry on their own. This stretcher will hold up to 300 pounds.
4693. A portable sweat lodge consisting of a large leather wrap containing a foldable wooden framework that sits on stilts, pliable slats of birch bark, additional leather rolls, and consecrated stones. When assembled, the resulting hut is a small dome, suitable as a tent shelter and large enough to fit two crouching adult humans. When the stones are heated and water is poured over them to create a thick steam, the hut becomes a sweat lodge for healing ceremonies.
4694. A portrait of a figure sitting astride one of the Great Desert Worms. The portrait changes over time so the figure resembles whoever possesses it.
4695. A portrait of an unsmiling woman painted on a poplar panel.
4696. A pottery vase decorated with images of a foot race.
4697. A pouch containing a dozen small semi-circular stones worn perfectly smooth. An esoteric rune depicting various types of magic adorns each stone.
4698. A pouch containing dried brown grass that when, smoked it gives off a pungent spicy aroma.
4699. A pouch filled with a dozen silver coins of great age, depicting forgotten gods and god-kings engaged in carnal acts.
4700. A pouch filled with a well-made wig with long, black hair and several pieces of gaudy fake jewellery.
4701. A pouch made of rough toad skin.
4702. A pouch of dried kelp filled with razor-sharp mollusk shells broken into pieces and tied together to act as an area denial weapon. The shells functions as caltrops in every respect.
4703. A pouch of dust that, when sprinkled onto a fire cause firework-like sparks and crackles
4704. A pouch of small medicinal vials labelled with different names, with a handwritten note detailing dosages and urgency in delivery.
4705. A pouch of very small bones that can be fitted together to form a strange, highly complex and obviously occult symbol.
4706. A pouch that holds nothing but ready-to-eat smoked bats’ wings. The pouch contains enough food to sustain a grown man for two days. The wings are chewy and bland except for a vaguely smoky aftertaste.
4707. A powerful magnet that appears to be in the shape of a stylized spider
4708. A powerful magnifying glass
4709. A practice longsword that is shaped like a true sword but is made of bundled wood lathes and is bound with cloth to reduce impact and the risk of injury.
4710. A preserved flower that changes to become the same color as the last meal of the creature wearing it
4711. A preserved flower that lights up when you blow on it
4712. A preserved human scalp.
4713. A preserved scalp complete from the top of the forehead to the base of the skull. It has long black hair braided half-way, then banded with three small gold rings inlaid with black iron characters.
4714. A primitive, hand sized wicker statuette, depicting a dark god. The idol glows a deep red whenever fresh blood is spilled on it.
4715. A promissory note to Ms Lorthe Toureme, entitling her to “…three of the finest racing steeds of Vervagen Steeds & Tannery.”
4716. A prosthetic bronze hand with ivory fingernails.
4717. A prosthetic eye made of ivory and set with an opal iris. The eye whispers unintelligible breathy words to the bearer in the dark and if worn during sleep, the bearer suffers from terrible, barely remembered Random Nightmares.
4718. A prosthetic hook hand that’s infamously used by pirates and corsairs.
4719. A psaltery made from the darkest ebony wood. Its back is slightly curved with an indentation in the base so that it sits nicely on the player’s lap. Inlayed in its face is a twisted branch covered in beautiful cherry blossoms. As the instrument is played the blossoms seem to fall away to reveal that the branch is not a branch at all but the bony hand of a skeleton.
4720. A puka shell necklace strung on a hemp cord meant to be worn tightly around the neck.
4721. A pulsing, mossy stone of unknown origin that glows with the light of life.
4722. A pure white face mask with pewter trim around its edges. It is sculpted into what could be called a bird head with an unusually short beak, also covered in pewter, but with delicate filigree.
4723. A purple amulet in the shape of a pig’s head.
4724. A purple apple that never rots or decays and tastes like saltwater when licked.
4725. A purple handkerchief embroidered with the name of a powerful archmage
4726. A purple silk scarf, bearing the insignia of House Ortesia.
4727. A purse made of alligator skin, containing 29 copper coins.
4728. A pyramid of sticky black incense that smells very bad
4729. A quartz and horn prayer beads on a silk cord.
4730. A quartz statuette of a pegasus taking flight.
4731. A queen piece from a chess set with a hidden compartment. Inside is a human finger bone.
4732. A quill and bottle of green ink that glows in the dark
4733. A quill and ink set, when used the quill sometimes (without the writers notice) writes dire portents of doom and death.
4734. A quill that stands on it’s tip, waiting to be used
4735. A quill with a small ink reserve carved into it.
4736. A rabbit felt wide brimmed, high crown fedora with a band around it. It looks dusty with age.
4737. A rabbit’s foot
4738. A ragged hunk of malachite has been broken open to reveal a series of translucent cyan domes. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as chrysocolla.
4739. A ragged piece of burlap with the personal crest of a wealthy merchant inked onto it.
4740. A ragged, thin tin box containing two long, steel nails. Each has a shaft convoluted with organic whorls and notches, and a head showing a carved rose or curling tongue. On the box’s lid is pasted a hand-drawn, musty sketch showing where the nails should be driven into a human’s skull.
4741. A randomly colored fist-sized cube that occasionally makes strange noises.
4742. A rank insignia from a lost legionnaire
4743. A ransom note from bandits who are holding the heir of a local lord captive. The note details their demands including where and when to bring the ransom treasure to make the exchange.
4744. A rare copper coin whose face shows an image of Old Man McLoyf, god of medicine and drink.
4745. A rat skeleton held together by copper wire, encased in a block of clear glass.
4746. A rather clunky cube of dark wood, engraved with hypnotizing patterns.
4747. A rather intricately filigreed belt buckle featuring a stylistic rendering of a heroic figure standing in defiance of a formless darkness looming above it.
4748. A rather large iron pot covered in scratches and scorch marks that can’t seem to be taken off. Meals prepared by this pot are always palatable but in need of salt, regardless of whether the recipe called for salt, the consumer’s enjoyment of salt, or amount of salt that was used in the cooking. Coincidentally, this effect can be nullified by adding copious amounts of pepper to the pot before serving the meal, in which case the food is always surprisingly delicious.
4749. A rattle shaped like the skull of a horned creature. Dried blood adds a macabre touch to the horns.
4750. A rattle that looks like a shrunken humanoid skull. Eyeball-like marbles rattle within it.
4751. A raven-like plague doctor’s mask
4752. A raven’s feather that is hot to the touch.
4753. A raw, unprocessed chunk of tourmaline that catches the and reflects different colors as it moves. It protrudes out of a base of stone and is flecked with dust and dirt.
4754. A razor sharp hunting knife with a gut hook.
4755. A razor-sharp black dragon claw still covered with ash from the flames that killed its late owner.
4756. A realistic looking toy tarrasque the size of a human hand. Clicking a button on it’s back causes it to loose a small roar and take three steps forward.
4757. A realistic looking, wooden theater dagger that resembles a sacrificial blade.
4758. A realistic painting of a manatee grazing in the shallow waters near a coast. The image of the sea cow and the water it’s swimming in, moves slowly within the frame.
4759. A receipt of deposit at a bank in a far-flung city
4760. A recently painted picture of an author believed to have disappeared fifty years previous.
4761. A recorder carved from brilliant white ash.
4762. A rectangular metal device with two tiny metal cups on one end that throws sparks when wet
4763. A rectangular mirror held in a frame of pale ashwood. Whenever a creature gazes into the the mirror, they perceive an elven version of their face in place of their own reflection.
4764. A rectangular wooden box labelled “Rawshins” containing dozens of red wax spheres. The balls have some give to them and the wax can be peeled away to reveal the pickled eye of a horse. The eyeballs while horrendously unpalatable is remarkable nutritious due to the herbal mixture used to preserve them and the box contains 2d4+1 days’ worth of trail rations.
4765. A red brass sphere, slightly pitted. It rings dully when shaken and feels as though some thick liquid or sand shifts inside. If shaken vigorously, it becomes chillingly cold. A shallow mark has been stamped on one side: A long triangle, perhaps a tooth or an icicle.
4766. A red building brick that sings in a strange language every six hours for a few minutes. The melody varies but the words always seem to be the same.
4767. A red gemstone that changes to a deep purple colour when it catches the light
4768. A red plate made of etched ceramic that, whenever something is eaten off of it a growling noise can be heard from somewhere nearby.
4769. A red potion flask fashioned in the shape of a bull filled with an amber liquid. If consumed, the drinker’s face turns red and he becomes unable to sleep or rest properly for 1d20 hours. This does no eliminate the drinker’s need for rest, it simply blocks their ability to do so.
4770. A red silk veil that was once worn by a genie.
4771. A red velvet drawstring pouch containing a dozen very small silver tokens, each with a happy face painted on them.
4772. A red whistle that makes no sound when blown, but does seem to calm rodents.
4773. A reel of thread that never frays.
4774. A regal pouch crafted from deep purple cloth with golden accents. It feels like there may be six small but solid objects resting at the bottom of the pouch. If emptied, several strange stones with straight edges and sharp vertices fall out. Upon examination it appears each of the stones has a series of numbers etched upon them. The owner may roll these royal dice for a chance of luck being bestowed upon them.
4775. A regular looking bronze coin that, when thrown, always lands on the edge.
4776. A relatively airtight waterskin that can be used as an air bladder for swimmers on longer dives. If inflated, it holds enough air to sustain an adult human for an additional 30 seconds.
4777. A religious icon made of gold, depicting a creature resembling a blasphemous crossing of man and sea-beast.
4778. A religious icon that once belonged to a distant ancestor.
4779. A repair kit for a bard’s lute including replacement strings and a cloth to clean the instrument.
4780. A rhinoceros shaped medallion, carved from the horn of the animal that it resembles.
4781. A rich, purple hand fan. Intricate designs of peacocks cover it’s surface when unfolded.
4782. A ring carved with the unfinished insignia to a defunct secret organization
4783. A ripped, light blue war banner, depicting a blazing white sun in its center.
4784. A roc feather fan
4785. A rock that, when handled, seems to crumble along the edges until it is worn down to a perfect resemblance of a human heart. When set down, it immediately returns to its original, featureless state
4786. A rock with a patch of curious purple moss that occasionally puffs out hallucinogenic spores.
4787. A rod that does nothing but make overly dramatic “SWOOSHing” noises at even the slightest movement.
4788. A rod that makes clicking sounds when wet
4789. A roll of bandages that has been inked with strange pictographs.
4790. A roll of five bandages that changes size to perfectly fit any wound up to six inches
4791. A roll of insect netting large enough to completely cover a grown man lying prone.
4792. A roll of old bandages that has been inked with strange pictograms.
4793. A rolled leather case containing a set of eight chisels used for stone masonry.
4794. A rolled scroll on which was hastily scribbled hymn, full of zeal. It says that it is only in rapturous frenzy that the spirit exhibits grace. At this moment, we are freed from doubt, acting only in accordance with the divine, immaculate will.
4795. A rolled up battle banner of a famous mercenary company that has long since disbanded. The material is old and weathered but could be restored.
4796. A rolled up canvas painting of a dreary field with dark uninviting woods beyond. The leaves of the trees in the background of the painting seem to sway and there appears to be something moving through the field.
4797. A rolled up parchment containing a handful of black hair, but no writing.
4798. A rolled up portrait of a bashful looking tiefling.
4799. A rolled up war banner of a noble house in the local kingdom.
4800. A rope necklace from which dangles four mummified fingers
4801. A rose quartz paperweight shaped like a crushing fist.
4802. A rose-cut golden opal wrapped carefully in twine.
4803. A rough bone carving of a golden dragon and a kobold, etched into the bottom is a name in Draconic: “Vimrul”.
4804. A rough map that shows the local area with an unfamiliar landmark.
4805. A rough-hewn, rust-colored stone filled with half-buried flakes of dark blue crystals forming the semblance of a rose. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as azurite.
4806. A rough, milky white gem with a red crystal grown directly through one side and tinged with yellow discolorations. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as realgar.
4807. A roughly circular slab of obsidian an inch thick, or thereabouts, and just the right size to be cradled comfortably in one’s palms. Roughly shaped around its circumference, though one broad side is simply stone-pecked to a slightly convex, pitted surface the other has been polished to such a high degree that the surface is unblemished as still water. Perfectly smooth, the polished side can act as a fine (If dark) mirror. Those who peer into the silky smooth reflective face of slab long enough, however, see floating within the midnight depths a rendition of the starry skies in smoky points of light. Focused on the zodiacal constellations, these tiny dark “stars” change with the day and the seasons in perfect step with those in the sky above.
4808. A roughly-polished stone that is incised with runes spelling out prayers to the god of earth and stone
4809. A round offering plate made of polished brass with a red felt pad attached to its interior. Markings on the underside of the deep plate real that it is the property of a local shrine.
4810. A round offering plate made of stainless steel with a blue felt pad attached to its interior. Markings on the underside of the deep plate real that it is the property of the large cathedral of a nearby kingdom.
4811. A round offering plate made of varnished maple with a green felt pad attached to its interior. Markings on the underside of the deep plate real that it is the property of a local shrine.
4812. A round, polished moonstone that, when peered through at a clear night sky, one can see any local constellations in sharp relief.
4813. A royal decree ordering all land-holding families to send one armed soldier to an official army muster. Any family that fails to respond is in danger of having their ancestral land titles revoked.
4814. A royal scepter made from the tip end of a spear, roughly two feet in length. It sports a green and white tassel beneath the spearhead, and has dragons carved into the remaining haft.
4815. A ruby the size of a man’s fist which sparkles and gleams with the promise of untold wealth in the faintest of lights, but turns to granite upon the lightest touch. It reverts back to a ruby after an hour of being left untouched.
4816. A rudimentary tripwire consisting of a length of thin string wrapped around two small sharpened, wooden stakes along with a length of tiny, ash-darkened, copper bells that can be wrapped around the tripwire. The trap can cover an area five feet in length.
4817. A rune covered monocle that allows the bearer to see into the ultraviolet spectrum.
4818. A rusted chainmail pouch which appears to have some sort of magical darkness inside it. All attempts to peer through the links fail unless a magical light source is shone into the pouch. Only then will the eyeball encased in resin within be visible. The eyeball belongs to a necromancer in a far off land. If removed from the pouch, the necromancer can see through the eye again.
4819. A rusted iron door knocker in the shape of a gargoyle. Its mouth is open and the knocker itself is held in it’s front claws. If the knocker is touched the gargoyle’s mouth grinds into movement and issues a verbal warning: “Disturb not this chamber, lest the world suffer!” The gargoyle can issue this warning once a minute.
4820. A rusted iron key with a tag that reads “Front Door”
4821. A rustic lute carved of driftwood engraved with images of ships and butts.
4822. A sack of glass marbles, with what looks like a starburst frozen inside each.
4823. A sack of Owlbear feathers.
4824. A sadistic violin that no matter how much it’s tuned, will always play terribly. The instrument seems to want to annoy and bring misery to as many people as it can. If anyone ever starts to actually enjoy the out of tune music, the violin will change to a different worse sound, ensuring no listener ever brought joy by its sounds.
4825. A satchel containing a cloth measuring tape, a hammer, saw, shovel, and a small box of nails. All of the objects, including the satchel are sized for a small child to use and a perceptive PC can find stitching on the interior of the satchel that reads “My Little Undertaker”.
4826. A satchel containing everything needed to care for metal weaponry, including whetstones, oil and assorted tools. They seem virtually new.
4827. A satchel made from the hide of a wearbear that contains a small ceramic jar filled with dried wolfsbane.
4828. A satyr statuette which increases the libido of everyone within line of sight of it.
4829. A scabbard of black ash and bronze decorated with a sneering bearded face with tourmaline eyes. It is suitable for longsword or similar straight blade.
4830. A scalpel with a wide blade, etched with a diagram of a human body on each side. One side features a chart of the circulatory system, and the other a layout of the muscles and tendons.
4831. A scarlet gem that shimmers in the bearer’s hand like the pale cinders of cooling hearth. The bearer can feel its brittle heat wash over them, seething through their veins like serpents of liquid steel.
4832. A scarlet sash woven of fine red thread and delicate gold wire.
4833. A scepter made with scorched wood, that has an orb of solid, coagulated blood on it’s edge.
4834. A scourge of red-dyed leather with iron filings entwined in the leather.
4835. A scrap of cloth from an old banner
4836. A scrap of dirty parchment bearing a list of names, some of them crossed off. Investigation reveals all of the names on the list are dead people, mostly buried in the Gilded Graveyard. Those who have been crossed off have recently have their graves’ plundered, their bodies stolen. Further investigation still reveals that these were all jurors in the trial of Isabella Rasping, a necromancer convicted of using a zombies as murder weapons during the infamous “Meatpuppet Murders” two centuries ago. She was executed for the crime by her own creations. Isabella has returned as a revenant with unfinished business; she maintains her innocence and believes she can now prove it, and so is gathering the previous jurors for a kind of “retrial.“
4837. A scrap of fabric that whispers the word ‘Liffen’ when rubbed
4838. A scrap of hide engraved with the first half of a child’s nursery rhyme.
4839. A scrap of paper that says, “Thank you for dealing with this ‘person.’ For a reward, please visit the Dancing Diva Festhall.” Under those words is a smudged, bright red lip imprint.
4840. A scrap of paper that words literally slide off of, no matter what they are written with.
4841. A scrap of parchment extolling the virtues of an evil cult’s dark lord. The signature of one of the cultists lies under a pledge of allegiance.
4842. A scrap of parchment that reads; “It is done. Meet me in the graveyard at dawn, near the crypt.”
4843. A scrap of parchment that reads; “Leave the jewel in a sewer grate by the church, or the next time you look into her eyes they won’t be in her head.”
4844. A scrap of parchment with detailed drawing of a demonic ritual circle
4845. A scribe’s fevered account of having watched a play that caused the audience to go berserk and attack each other, although the scribe claims to not remember the contents of the play.
4846. A script for a never-published play about an author going mad, the writing gets noticeably less neat and more frantic, eventually stopped before the final page.
4847. A scroll case containing a scroll scribed with an unfinished spell.
4848. A scroll case containing a set of local astrological charts
4849. A scroll case made of sewn snakeskin and metal plates.
4850. A scroll case with a hidden compartment. Inside rests a single piece of vellum which holds the names of a dozen individuals; only four have not been crossed out.
4851. A scroll containing a family tree showing the seven male heirs of a noble family, there is a red circle drawn around the name of the seventh son.
4852. A scroll containing the secret recipe for the most popular ale in the realm.
4853. A scroll covered with depictions of constellations.
4854. A scroll of complex formulae and charcoal sketches, depicting some manner of winged flying machine.
4855. A scroll that chronicles the adventures of Zhataru, an infamous thief of legend who vanished mysteriously.
4856. A scroll that repeats these words endlessly; “Seek out the Gilded Glade and place me upon the pedestal.”
4857. A scroll titled: “The Rules and Regulations for Properly Attacking, Subduing, and Dispatching a Dragon of Any Solid Colour by Lord Pepping IV. Knowledgeable PC’s will remember that Lord Pepping was killed and eaten by a green dragon.
4858. A scroll tube containing a care-worn love letter the bearer has read and refolded so many times it is starting to fall apart. The signature at the bottom is smeared and impossible to read. The letter explains the parting of a human (Most likely the bard) and their elven lover. The elf believed the relationship was doomed because of the tragically short life span of humans and refused to commit to such a short relationship.
4859. A scroll tube containing a list of the PC’s names along with brief descriptions of their appearances and assumed abilities.
4860. A scroll tube containing a set of documents verifying the (Unnamed and vaguely described) bearer is an official emissary of a nearby kingdom.
4861. A scroll tube containing several scraps of paper that name the PCs and provide rough descriptions of their appearance and abilities. Perceptive PC’s will realize that the notes (given the different handwriting styles) seem to have been written by at least three different people.
4862. A scroll tube with the symbol of an hourglass etched into its length. It contains a painting of what one of the PC’s would look like if they were much older.
4863. A scroll with the epic poem “The Canticle of the Dragon” written in expertly flowing calligraphy.
4864. A scroll written in abyssal on a piece of demon-leather. The scroll is bound with a carved jade ring shaped into the likeness of a demon’s face. The scroll seems to be a ledger on the souls the demon has yet to collect.
4865. A sea captain’s hat with the name “Sexy Captain Alice” sewn into the inner lining.
4866. A sea dragon’s tooth that always feels wet and smells like the ocean.
4867. A seal stamp made of dark green stone that is always cool to the touch. The seal is a leafless tree.
4868. A sealed cedar box containing a dozen good quality smoking cigars.
4869. A sealed clay jar painted with a crude cornucopia spilling out its contents and contains half a pound of mixed spices.
4870. A sealed clay jar painted with a crude ocean scene and contains half a pound of coarse sea salt
4871. A sealed clay jar with a fat bumble bee painted on it. The jar contains half a pound of pure, strong smelling beeswax.
4872. A sealed clay pot painted with various symbols of love and fertility, that contains a pound of an aphrodisiac root.
4873. A sealed clear glass bubble containing an orange and green frog being kept alive by magic. It moves around, sleep and seems to be aware of its surroundings but never needs to eat or drink and does not age.
4874. A sealed envelope made of red leather that’s completely unopenable. It smells of campfire.
4875. A sealed glass bottle filled with virgin olive oil
4876. A sealed glass bottle labeled “Doctor John’s Cod Liver Oil”. The bottle is filled with a clear liquid that is so pure and so strong.
4877. A sealed glass bottle of absinthe with the image of a small fairy etched on the glass. This green alcoholic drink, made from wormwood, is rumored to enhance creativity, which makes it a favored beverage of artists and eccentrics.
4878. A sealed glass jar filled with embalming fluid in which floats a perfectly preserved wyvern fetus
4879. A sealed glass jar filled with sugar cubes.
4880. A sealed glass jar with a live butterfly inside of it. The blue insect flutters around occasionally and seems to be in good spirits. The jar cannot be unsealed but the butterfly never seems to suffer from lack of fresh air or food and never dies.
4881. A sealed glass petri dish holding a small ooze like substance labeled, “Experiment.
4882. A sealed glass sphere three inches in diameter, containing water and and decorative fish that drift around the enclosure.
4883. A sealed glass vial containing a fishhook-sized barb from the stinger of a giant bee
4884. A sealed jar with a slab of fresh honeycomb inside
4885. A sealed letter addressed to a military general that died several years ago
4886. A sealed letter containing the deed to The Hunting Harlot in Port Judewater.
4887. A sealed letter ordering the execution of one of the PC’s and stamped with various official seals.
4888. A sealed metal tin labeled “Armstrong Mustache Wax”. According to the description, the recipe has been passed down the Armstrong line for generations
4889. A sealed one gallon cask filled with a smoky, spicy spirit akin to weaker tequila. This aperitif is made from a flowering cactus found deep in the deserts heart. When drunk, it causes memories to flow more freely to the drinker’s minds forefront, often sparking intense feelings of nostalgia or regret. If overindulged, it could even dislodged repressed memories, forgotten dreams, and other things forgotten (deliberately or otherwise).
4890. A sealed one gallon cask of applejack. This even stronger version of hard cider is typically made by allowing hard cider to freeze during the winter cold, then removing the ice to extract much of the water from the cider and concentrate the alcohol.
4891. A sealed one gallon cask of Baijiu. This clear alcoholic beverage, distilled from sorghum, is extremely potent, and is often regarded as an acquired taste due to its corrosive flavor.
4892. A sealed one gallon cask of dwarven stout. More a family of beers than one single drink, dwarven stouts are dark beers characterized by a slightly burnt flavor and a foamy head, they are said to be as filling as a meal.
4893. A sealed one gallon cask of finely aged wine that gives the imbiber prophetic, drunken visions.
4894. A sealed one gallon cask of Scrumble. The brandy known as Scrumble is made from apples… Well, mainly apples, which in no way guarantees a low alcohol content. Scrumble is drunk in thimblefuls, is strong enough to be used for cleaning spoons, and should not be put in a metal container or allowed to come into contact with water.
4895. A sealed red envelop that reads, “FOR RECIPIENT’S EYES ONLY. DELIVER TO 2323 SCION’S CIRCLE IMMEDIATELY.”
4896. A sealed scrollcase containing a stack of fine vellum.
4897. A sealed tin of quality chewing tobacco
4898. A sealed tin of roasted bitter black beans. Chewing on the beans or grinding them up and boiling them in a type of black tea will grant the consumer one additional hour of wakefulness allowing them to delay (But not ignore) the effects of sleep or exhaustion. There are enough beans for ten cups worth.
4899. A sealed vessel topped by a baboon’s head, all crafted of glazed porcelain. The canopic jar contains a wrapped and preserved pair of humanoid lungs.
4900. A sealed vessel topped by a falcon’s head, all crafted of glazed porcelain. The canopic jar contains a wrapped and preserved humanoid intestines.
4901. A sealed vessel topped by a jackal’s head, all crafted of glazed porcelain. The canopic jar contains a wrapped and preserved humanoid stomach.
4902. A sealed vessel topped by a mummy’s head, all crafted of glazed porcelain. The canopic jar contains a wrapped and preserved humanoid liver.
4903. A sealed wooden box holding seven sealed scrolls containing missives to seven different people. The missives are written in code and need to be deciphered.
4904. A sealing stamp bearing the emblem of a wolf’s head.
4905. A seashell that when held to the ear, emits sounds like the howling wind.
4906. A seed pod that becomes an edible fruit when dipped in water.
4907. A seemingly normal conch shell. When pressed to the ear, faint sounds of surf and wind, rustling palms and crying gulls can be heard. The area around the listener’s ear is specked with sand afterwards.
4908. A seemingly ordinary grocery list except for next to last item, “1 pound elf flesh”.
4909. A selection of maps, all rolled tightly together, and crammed into one tube. The maps all show the expansion of the same location over a period of 60 years, one new map every 10 years.
4910. A sequined squid skin belt pouch.
4911. A series of ancient, hand-drawn maps representing uncountable countries and territories in some unknown part of the world.
4912. A series of five vials in a wooden box, each filled with a bright green liquid. Four of them contain a sweet tasting liquid, and one of them contains a horrid and acrid liquid that deals acid damage equivalent to a shortsword when consumed in any quantity. They can only be distinguished by taste. The box reads: “Game of Chance”.
4913. A series of fortune-tellers ivory tiles with letters from the common alphabet inked onto them.
4914. A series of sharp teeth on a leather strap, which can be affixed around the head. A realistic yellow cat eye blinks in the central position.
4915. A serviceable hacksaw dotted with flecks of rust.
4916. A set of a dozen silver nails.
4917. A set of bagpipes made from a rich mahogany wood, artfully carved and well balanced.
4918. A set of bagpipes made from the skin of a displacer beast, with the drones and chanter carved from its bones.
4919. A set of bamboo windchimes.
4920. A set of beautiful wind chimes made from shells and bones of a Yeshen, a small aquatic lizard found on the banks of the River Oceanus within the plane of Elysium. Yeshen shells are similar to a conch shells in their shape, but their swirling colors vary from midnight blue to a golden red hue. The shells are tuned by carving holes or slits into them; as the shell turns, different notes are played.
4921. A set of bells of different sizes that plays a scale from high to low and the bells go from large to small
4922. A set of blueprints and plans for the castle of the local kingdom, revealing both the location of the treasury and a secret door in the queen’s bedchamber leading to a secret dungeon below. The blueprints seem authentic, but it’s impossible to verify the truth without investigating in person.
4923. A set of bone dice that emit an eerie cackling when rolled.
4924. A set of bone pipes
4925. A set of bongos made of elephant hide stretched over alder.
4926. A set of brass merchant weigh scales that have subtle markings around the balancing arm. An extremely perceptive PC will notice that the markings are actually small levels that can be quickly manipulated with the lightest touch in order to cause the scales to tip more in one direction or the other making it seems like one scale weighs more than it rightly should. No doubt owned by a crooked merchant, the fraudulent mechanisms are quite well made and would fetch a fine prince if one could find a shady black marketeer or underhanded jeweler.
4927. A set of brass wind chimes that glow when the wind blows.
4928. A set of chess pieces carved from ivory.
4929. A set of child’s toy tools made of cork. The set consists of a hammer, pry bar, screwdrivers, and a strip of measuring cloth.
4930. A set of childs wooden building blocks, when left on their own unobserved they occasional spell out words.
4931. A set of chimes made from hollowed bones. When hung up on a leather cord they make a low whistling sound when the wind hits them right.
4932. A set of clear glass spectacles in a wire frame that give everything an amber hue when worn.
4933. A set of cloth pennants on a tall staff. When the wind blows them, the long flags of dark blues and blacks speckled with silver spots, resemble nothing more than a banner of the night sky.
4934. A set of collapsible opera glasses.
4935. A set of colourful glass beads on a silken cord, designed to be worn as hair ornamentation.
4936. A set of comfortable house slippers that keep the bearer’s feet at an ideal temperature.
4937. A set of cymbals made out of thinned Dwarven shields that saw their share of combat.
4938. A set of dark wooden pipes that produce a low moaning sound when used.
4939. A set of dice carved from white dragon bone. They will chill small quantities of liquid if placed inside a vessel of any kind. If rolled the dice will cover a small surface in a thin layer of ice.
4940. A set of dominoes inscribed with unholy runes rather than pips
4941. A set of fake eyelashes made out of owlbear fur.
4942. A set of fancifully etched translucent yellow wineglasses that flash brilliant fluorescent green when they catch the sun.
4943. A set of fine drawing pencils crafted from yew.
4944. A set of fine, angular robes closed with a wide band, tied behind him in a crisp, elaborate knot. The clothing is impeccably clean and its former owner must have been fastidious about his appearance.
4945. A set of fish jaws fashioned into a bracelet. When worn, the bearer has an in depth knowledge of northern pike.
4946. A set of five bones wrapped with different-hued ribbons. Each ribbon is made from different fabric, and each one causes the bearer to smell or taste something unsavory when touched.
4947. A set of five dice with elemental symbols and primordial runes instead of pips or numbers.
4948. A set of five juggling balls made of hollow wicker, filled with metal bells that chime when tossed around.
4949. A set of flint and steel in an old calfskin wrap with the tail still attached held in a wool holdall.
4950. A set of four brightly colored juggling sticks adorned with colorful streamers that can be tossed and manipulated to create displays and patterns.
4951. A set of four ceramic canopic jars, holding the preserved stomach, intestines, lungs, and liver of a long-ago monarch. Each lid is covered in gold leaf and stamped with the likeness of a paunchy, hook-nosed man with pale skin and a squint.
4952. A set of four copper pots of various sizes.
4953. A set of four horseshoes that seem to be magnetically attracted to hooves, requiring no additional fastening.
4954. A set of four juggling balls each made of a different Random Colour of glass.
4955. A set of four lovingly polished, maple wood coasters with green felt grips underneath.
4956. A set of four metallic, pointed-toe boots designed to fit a horse or a similar equine creature
4957. A set of glass playing cards in a brass case. Each card has a set of symbols and numerals unknown to scholars and the learned.
4958. A set of goggles meant to reduce sun glare, carved from a single piece of bone that been shaped to fit the bearer’s face, with two narrow horizontal slits carved through the front. Two small holes were bored on either end of the goggles and a leather strap is used to tighten the goggles to the wearer. The goggles fit tightly against the face so that the only light entering is through the slits and soot was applied to the inside to further cut down on glare. The goggles reduce wearer’s field of vision by half vertically (The bearer cannot see the ground immediately in front of them or upwards directly above them, if staring forward) but greatly increases their tolerance for bright light.
4959. A set of gray and red robes with threadwork made to resemble teeth and mouths around all of the openings. The sleeves and wristcuffs always stretch until they extend past the bearer’s hands making it seem like objects he holds are being eaten and swallowed.
4960. A set of half a dozen tiny figurines carved into simple animal shapes. When placed on a reasonably flat surface or held in the palm of your hand, they gradually begin to animate and interact with one another.
4961. A set of haunting sheet music labelled as ‘Rudolpho’s Concerto of Madness.’
4962. A set of hunting knives that are in pristine condition except for the gnaw marks on the handles.
4963. A set of ivory runes believed to aid in fortune-telling.
4964. A set of juggling balls made from dried lamb heads.
4965. A set of leather riding gloves with the initials J. M. branded onto them.
4966. A set of leather saddlebags with two concealed pockets inside them.
4967. A set of make-up in various skin tones.
4968. A set of monk’s prayer beads consisting of a dozen or so colourless pearls and a single runed bead. If a tear of sorrow touches a bead, it turns a deep shade of blue forever; similarly, a tear of joy turns the pearl golden. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize the runed bead as originating from an order of monks who grant this string to their new followers. The acolyte is supposed to alternate blue and golden pearls through their life, so that they will always remember while praying that sorrow must follow joy and joy must follow sorrow.
4969. A set of one inch lead figurines that look strikingly similar to the PCs
4970. A set of panpipes fashioned from a tree struck by lightning.
4971. A set of pewter tankards, five in all, which have various pictures of historic castles and their coats of arms. On the back of each tankard is a verse which, if deciphered, will reveal a ribald and amusing fact about the holders of the coats of arms.
4972. A set of plans and blueprints for the largest structure in the local area
4973. A set of plans and blueprints for the Sailing Chariot of Stevenus, a wind-powered land vehicle capable of carrying up to six grown men. According to the notes it only really works on windy days across hard flat plains.
4974. A set of portable war drums made of wood and taut dragon-skin
4975. A set of prayer beads consisting of nearly two dozen human teeth with tiny evil runes etched into them. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that strings like these are a popular focus for prayers among flesh cults.
4976. A set of reusable, long, fake nails that are made of turquoise.
4977. A set of robes primarily red in colour, with subtle green highlights, as well as silver thread embroidery and grey fur lining around the collar, hood and sleeves. Overall it seems to border a fine line between looks and utility, given that the sleeves feature each a strap allowing them to be rolled up and secured, and several leather belts attached to the inside of the robe fill in the role of pockets or holsters. Under direct sunlight, the robes have a very faint, barely noticeable iridescent sheen to them.
4978. A set of seven humanoid shaped obsidian pendants.
4979. A set of sheet music for a lost operetta composed by a respected composer.
4980. A set of sheet music for a popular folk song adapting it to tablature needed for a three-handed mandolin player.
4981. A set of sheet music to a song that cannot be played on any mortal instrument.
4982. A set of six sided dice with lewd pictures on each of the faces.
4983. A set of six wooden nesting dolls whose faces become more terrified the smaller they get
4984. A set of six, six-sided dice made of black painted wood with white pips. When rolled individually or as a group they always add up to prime numbers.
4985. A set of six, six-sided dice made of white painted wood with black pips. When rolled individually, or as a group they never add up to prime numbers.
4986. A set of sky blue robes made from a high quality cotton with the Order of Deacons symbol sewn into the chest.
4987. A set of smooth iron bangles. They have a decent weight to them, as if they’re pure rather than plated.
4988. A set of soft leather suspenders that fasten to buttons on the front and back of trousers. They have two adjustable iron clips, triangular in shape and heavily rusted, each bearing an etched diamond shape.
4989. A set of soldier’s studded red leather greaves that come up over the knees and cling tightly to the calf. There are no visible closures or bindings on the armor. The red leather is artfully burned with the pattern of twining vines. To remove the armor, a command word must be whispered which awakens the vines and relaxes them, allowing the greaves to slip off easily and quickly. To don them, the same verbal command must be uttered causing the vines tighten and recess into the leather once again.
4990. A set of stone divining tiles used by shaman for foretelling the future.
4991. A set of sturdy, weatherproof saddlebags that can be draped over a saddle or mount to add extra carrying capacity. Each side of a saddlebag can typically carry 20 pounds of items that can fit in the various pouches.
4992. A set of tarot cards depicting each figure on the cards as a skeleton.
4993. A set of tassels that attach to your clothing and flutter when you’re near someone you love
4994. A set of taxidermist’s tools in a leather case, consisting of leather needles and punches, a pair of leather shears, a scraping razor and a fine-blade knife.
4995. A set of technical diagrams to create a weapon of unspeakable power. The diagrams are hundreds of years old and show how to make a trebuchet.
4996. A set of ten leather sleeves that fit over one’s fingertips and extend right up to the knuckles and palm.
4997. A set of thieves’ tools that includes a small file, a set of lock picks, a small mirror mounted on a metal handle, a set of narrow-bladed scissors, and a pair of pliers.
4998. A set of three palm sized brass monkeys, each one covering either their ears, mouth or eyes.
4999. A set of three porcelain ewers in blue flowers on a white field with silver trimming along the rim. A fourth, the largest, is shattered, but the silver is salvageable.
5000. A set of three small, geometric stones; each about two inches long with runes carved upon them. The first is red marble in the shape of a sphere. The second is white jade in the form of a spindle. The third is a purple crystal prism. These are Ioun Stones awaiting enchantment.
5001. A set of used dentures made of real human teeth. Four of the teeth have gold fillings.
5002. A set of very accurate weights and scales.
5003. A set of watercolor brushes perfectly sized for a gnome.
5004. A set of well made carpenter’s tools made for easy transport.
5005. A set of well made stone mason’s tools made for easy transport.
5006. A set of windchimes that move though no wind is present. The mellow sounds of their chimes bring back memories that make you ache with anguish and despair.
5007. A set of wood and leather sandals that appear sized for a halfling, gnome or small child.
5008. A set of wooden wind chimes which plays a different tune depending upon the direction of the wind which disturbs it.
5009. A set of worn prayer beads made of bone and barbed wire with numerous holy symbols of various Random Gods of Evil Domains. The spikes of the barbed wire are stained with dried blood. Using the beads as a focus for prayer may provide a measure of power to those who are ruthless enough to achieve it no matter the cost.
5010. A set of worn prayer beads made of wood and hemp with numerous holy symbols of various Random Gods of Good Domains. Using the beads as a focus for prayer may provide a measure of comfort to those who are truly deserving.
5011. A severed and preserved ape arm covered in black fur.
5012. A severed finger, still wearing a large jade ring
5013. A severed humanoid foot. It doesn’t bleed but moves as if it’s being tickled if touched.
5014. A severed rabbit’s foot strung on a thin brass chain. Although separated from the rest of the rabbit, the limb has been magically enchanted against death. It respond positively to being softly stroked and petted, freezes in place in response to loud noises and strong vibrations and even bleeds if injured and will attempt to kick the injuring party and escape in a hopping fashion. Well adjusted, sane, mentally stable creatures consider this object extremely disturbing and an abomination against the Gods at most, and good taste at least.
5015. A sewing kit filled with cacti needles with fine holes in the end instead of regular sewing needles.
5016. A sewing needle made from some type of unknown, otherworldly metal.
5017. A sewing thimble that, when poked by a needle, will roughly squeeze the bearer’s thumb.
5018. A shard of a mirror that shows strange shadows in its reflections.
5019. A shard of glass that floats a few inches off of the ground when near an open flame.
5020. A shard of glass. When looking through it, everything seen in ruins as if a great disaster has been brought about.
5021. A shard of obsidian that always feels warm to the touch
5022. A sharp pen, long, and crafted in smooth onyx. It will bear no ink on its piercing nib; only human blood. The silver nib will neither rust nor tarnish.
5023. A sharp sickle with dried blood covering its blade. Wisps of blond hair are stuck in the blood.
5024. A sharp tooth as long as a human hand. Looking at it makes a humanoid creature uneasy in a deeply primal way.
5025. A sharp, double-ended prong that is approximately nine inches in length and fitted with a stout leather neckband. A knowledgeable character will recognize this as a heretic’s fork, a nefarious device is secured on a helpless target’s neck so the prongs pierce both the underside of the victim’s chin and the victim’s chest. If the victim begins to fall asleep the prongs jab deeper into flesh, effectively keeping the victim awake indefinitely and subjecting the victim to fatigue and exhaustion through sleep deprivation.
5026. A shattered magenta stone with two dark green edges and that vaguely resembles sliced fruit. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as watermelon tourmaline.
5027. A shattered mask, once belonging to an ecclesiastic of the occult. Though broken this mask still retains a trace of its original purpose. It hums with faint whispers when worn. They demand an offering.
5028. A shattered stub of a wooden stake. Black blood covers the stake’s tip. Barely visible under the blood is some kind of rune, but its meaning is impossible to determine as part of it is missing.
5029. A shawl made from hundreds of colorful bird feathers
5030. A shawl of beautiful rainbow color with lace frilled edges resembling butts.
5031. A sheaf of loose leaf music written by the infamous conjurer, Wyrdlbr the Composarch.
5032. A sheaf of parchment that contains entirely too many limericks.
5033. A sheet of paper that absorbs ink (and stains) after one hour
5034. A sheet of parchment upon which is drawn a complex mechanical contraption
5035. A sheet of parchment with a black handprint and the words We Know” written underneath it.
5036. A sheet of tin rolled up into a scroll bound with platinum cord. The scroll must be unwound to be read, and reveals a chronological star chart, annotated in an alien language. If translated, the chart suggests a once in 433 year planetary alignment.
5037. A sheet of white canvas 2’ X 3’ stretched on a wooden frame. The painting is entirely taken up with the scrawling phrase “SUFFERING IS NOT ART!” which was apparently written in the painter’s own blood as some sort of artistic statement.
5038. A shifting monochromatic geometric, glass prism.
5039. A shimmering, clean yellow robe made of the lightest silk. Runes skitter across it and vanish. It smells of musk and roses.
5040. A shimmering, heavy bronze coin with unfamiliar writing on it.
5041. A shimmery cloak clasp depicting a violin and a sword. It smells vaguely of ash and fire.
5042. A shining baldric that seems to be woven from threads of steel, a skill only the finest of elven smiths have accomplished. Its peculiarly angled hanger is designed to carry an elven longsword.
5043. A shining bronze circlet, wrought in the shape of a laurel wreath. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as the third place trophy of the annual sporting competition of a local city.
5044. A shining steel bracelet etched with the phrase “Est Sularus oth Mithas”.
5045. A shiny brass locket with a portrait of a family of halflings in it
5046. A shiny leather doublets trimmed with seal fur and belted with checkered woollen sashes.
5047. A shiny steel sheriff’s badge of the local constabulary
5048. A ship in a bottle suspended above water that sloshes and froths rhythmically, regardless of whether or not it is moved or shaken. The ship bobs cheerfully in the water and is relaxing to look upon.
5049. A shipwreck in a water-filled glass globe. At the bottom is a massive kraken with tentacles up through the water. The ship is in big pieces that float with different levels of buoyancy. There are tiny sailors that float and sink to the bottom.
5050. A short cylindrical peakless hat made of thick red felt, with a tassel attached to the top.
5051. A short haired wig made from owlbear fur.
5052. A short necked, round bottom flask that could hold about a half-gallon of liquid. Clearly visible through the glass sides, though, is a city. When viewed extremely closely, such as under a magnifying glass, what appears to be tiny people walk through the streets, conducting their daily lives. No matter how the bottle is moved or tilted, those within don’t seem disturbed.
5053. A short scroll made out of singed papyrus, covered in strange, flowing script at crazy, disjointed angles and stains of ominous origin.
5054. A short scroll wrapped around a pair of dowels, bearing runic script and celestial patterns painted in rich, bold inks.
5055. A short wand made of pink crystal with a clear quartz tip. The wand has no magical properties but could yet be enchanted with some.
5056. A shoulder bag of great age with ornate decorations made from human finger bones.
5057. A shrunken frozen head of an arctic tribesman. No matter the temperature it does not defrost.
5058. A shrunken head of a zombie which still occasionally snaps its teeth together and moans.
5059. A shrunken head of an unknown humanoid. It looks happy.
5060. A shrunken head that occasionally whispers in an incomprehensible language.
5061. A shuttered bullseye lantern shaped like a screaming mouth. It contains a stubby black candle that burns far slower that should be possible.
5062. A signal horn fashioned from the thinly carved bone of a storm giant. Those who put their ear to the horn hear it whisper the sounds of an unknown village square. Whether the village which they hear is (Or was) a real place is as yet unknown.
5063. A signal horn that was made from the twisted shell of a burrowing creature.
5064. A signal horn that, when blown, blows bubbles instead of making a sound
5065. A silk bag with drawstring that open easily, revealing a glint of white. Inside is an elegant bone reliquary, smooth and pleasing to the touch. Polished, silvered fingerbones interlace to form a simple gate, operated by twisting a knob at the top formed from a single smooth vertebra surrounding a porcelain mechanism. Inside the small cavity is a cage formed out of rib that could have held an ancient curiosity of some sort, but now lies empty. The faintest touch of necromancy suffuses the curio, but surely any power it once held has long faded…
5066. A silk handkerchief printed with a grid of patterned hexagons.
5067. A silk headband decorated with a single green cat’s eye gem in its center. The bearer sometimes feels as though they can access another sense (Like telepathy, mind reading or telekinesis) focused through the gem but the sensation always fades before the bearer can make use of it
5068. A silk robe, dyed blood red with extremely long sleeves that hang past the hands, down to the knees. The outside is plain, but the inside reveals a subtle motif woven with orange threads: a nightingale swallowing a fox. Small, jingly bells hang from the hem.
5069. A silk rope tied into a monkey fist knot the size of an apple.
5070. A silk scarf once used to gag a captive siren. It occasionally makes strangely attractive sounds when the material rubs against itself
5071. A silken caul hair net decorated with small semiprecious stones.
5072. A silken veil that glistens in strange colours, like oil on water.
5073. A silver and brass mirror with lewd designs hidden around the frame.
5074. A silver badge in the shape of a five-pointed star
5075. A silver bracelet with finely-wrought filigree in the shape of vines.
5076. A silver brooch shaped like an imperious face framed by stylized, curling locks. It has small, yellow garnets for eyes. The eyes seem to be backed by mirrors, for they flash with an unusual brightness while in light. The brooch is magnetic, on its iron back, and connects powerfully to metal objects.
5077. A silver chain on which hangs a polished silver bell the size of a thimble. At one point in the bearer’s life, the bell will ring three times, producing three bursts of silvery sound with a brief silence after each. The vibrations that shiver through the bearer’s body will seem much too strong to have been produced by such a tiny clapper. This squared trio of ringing heralds that great danger will soon befall the bearer from which he is unlikely to survive.
5078. A silver charm bracelet with small kitsunes holding up different types of gemstones as if presenting a gift.
5079. A silver cloak pin styled in the shape of a dragon’s head.
5080. A silver coin stamped with a worn, unfamiliar design of a hawk-winged helm
5081. A silver coin that glows red when exposed to methane.
5082. A silver coin that makes no noise when dropped or hit against something
5083. A silver coin which has been hollowed out and a tiny encrypted message placed inside.
5084. A silver coin with a siren on heads and a banshee on tails. When the coin is flipped it will make ominous wailing sounds until the outcome is revealed.
5085. A silver coin with an engraved human that continuously waves to the bearer.
5086. A silver crossbow bolt with a cross engraved into it.
5087. A silver dragon scale that glows in the moonlight.
5088. A silver flask half filled with fine scotch, bearing a leering face engraved on the front.
5089. A silver hair pin.
5090. A silver hand mirror ornately engraved on both sides; one side bears a frowning mask, while the opposite bears a smiling one. There doesn’t appear to be an actual mirrored surface, despite its shape.
5091. A silver hilt of a sword with a grip of black leather. The blade is missing, or perhaps never existed to begin with. One night per year however, on the eve of the new year, the hilt manifests a ghostly blade for a single hour, allowing it to perform its duties as a sword once more.
5092. A silver holy symbol sculpted to resemble a shining sun. Such an image is sometimes used by clerics and paladins not associated with any particular deity. The amulet is small enough to be gripped in one hand and a religious bearer can feel that it contains the divine spark of a truly holy object.
5093. A silver horseshoe with foreign writing etched on to the side of the shoe. Roughly translates to “Trailfire”.
5094. A silver key of unknown origins, strung on a leather cord.
5095. A silver monstrance, set with gold detail, intricate in its design and covered with tiny curlicues that resemble angelic beings.
5096. A silver needle that always remains sharp but can puncture nothing
5097. A silver oak leaf cloak clasp, edged and highlighted with gold.
5098. A silver pocket watch as thick and round as a pomegranate that makes a sound like a bag of coins when moved. The delicate crystal face is shattered, and tiny gears and wheels skitter and jumble randomly within the interior.
5099. A silver skull the size of a coin
5100. A silver snuff box etched with a portrait of the night sky.
5101. A silver spoon with a handle shaped like a gorgon
5102. A silver spoon with an M engraved on the handle
5103. A silver teardrop earring made from a real teardrop
5104. A silver thimble containing a shimmering ballgown of spun moonlight. The ballgown is … very see through, but can be worn over another nice dress of plain material to good effect.
5105. A silver thimble engraved with the image of a rose.
5106. A silver thimble.
5107. A silver tube, long, worked with branchwork, and thin, filled with yellow powder. Said powder smells of hickory and some astringent tang. It fills the tube, which is closed with a screw cap, to the brim.
5108. A silver turtle shaped locket with a black opal shell. Inside the locket is a picture of somebody’s loved one.
5109. A silver whistle in the shape of a wolf’s head.
5110. A silver-like paintbrush with bone-white bristles ends on the other side with a small filament connected to a needle that must be pierced into the vein of its wielder. The paintbrush draws the blood out of the potential artist and channels it into an inner receptacle then into the bristle allowing the artist to easily paint in their own fresh blood or that of another.
5111. A silver-tipped quill with a brimming white feather.
5112. A simple brass bell that when rung, mimics the sound of a goat bleating.
5113. A simple but finely crafted leather armband embossed with a branch-like pattern.
5114. A simple chalkboard consisting of a book-sized wooden frame surrounding a thin, rectangular slice of flat black slate.
5115. A simple chunk of flint broken off of a larger rock eons ago by natural forces. A closer inspection, however, reveals one edge of the rock has been carefully napped down to a razor edge, while the opposite side has been shaped into a crude handgrip. Small, primitive figures of deer, wolves, and bears are etched into the stone. The carvings sometimes appear to have changed places of their own accord, though they never move while being observed.
5116. A simple clay pin in the shape of a human face. Its eyes dart about and its mouth moves as though attempting to speak.
5117. A simple elm wand, decorated with three red hawk feathers
5118. A simple folding chair made of a wooden frame and canvas seat and back, making it easy to transport and set up. It folds up easily, fits in a handy carrying bag and can support up to 250 pounds with ease.
5119. A simple framed painting of a figure leaning back in a chair, it’s face shrouded in shadow.
5120. A simple lantern with a hood covering. The hood spins as the base plays a music box tune to reveal pictures of clawed monsters, winged demons, witches and wolves on the wall. Different hoods can be placed on to show different scenes.
5121. A simple leather pouch that has several charred humanoid bones in it and a small dusting of ashes. There is no smell of smoke though, so they must be quite old.
5122. A simple looking box made of plain, unstained and untreated pine boards. The box is two feet high, three feet wide and one foot deep, and has horrific pictures of undead silhouettes burned on the outside of it. The lid is attached by a simple brass hinge and bears foul necromantic symbols. Inside, the box holds ashes that look suspiciously like cremation remains.
5123. A simple necromancer’s staff with an ornate head as dark as onyx and decorated with arcane symbols designed to prey on the subconscious fears of mortals.
5124. A simple quartz crystal with a series of emerald green and brown straws extending from within its central structure. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as tourmaline in quartz.
5125. A simple razor blade, used for shaving, resting on a pile of ash. A single drop of fresh blood marrs the perfect sheen of the blade.
5126. A simple silver ewer etched with a floral pattern.
5127. A simple silver fork. Embossed into the handle are the letters “JA.” The tines of the eating implement give off a slight green glow. A crowded inscription on the back of the fork reads: “To King Ragnis, may venom never touch thine lips.”
5128. A simple silver key with “Guest Room” etched finely on the handle.
5129. A simple silver mirror that shows a perfect reflection delayed by several seconds.
5130. A simple steel box that’s impossible to open except by a divine being.
5131. A simple white ribbon. While it is attached to clothing, the bearer finds it difficult to fall asleep.
