5683. A stoppered, green glass bottle wrapped in grimy stained leather and cord. It is filled with an inferior moonshine containing alcohol distilled in the worst possible conditions. The liquor tastes worse than it looks, but provides a small degree of resistance to the horrors of daily life
5684. A strand of ten flat black stones on a knotted leather thong, each carved with the “Yr” rune and roughly the size of a typical gold piece.
5685. A strange barbed choker that makes the bearer’s voice more resonant
5686. A strange flute of rubbery blue fibers interspaced with five holes. If played as an instrument by one skilled enough, it can replicate the unearthly music of fey creatures.
5687. A strange horn made of a winding pretzel of valves and tubes that according to the maker’s mark, was finely crafted by a powerful bard. Knowledgeable PC’s will remember that the horn was constructed for one purpose, to lock a terrible beast away deep within the mountain of Redwall. It contains a large portion of the life essence of that bard and to this day it remains as the solitary key to the door that holds the beast at bay. No one knows who this bard was, but he remains an unsung hero of the city.
5688. A strange idol of blackened glass that sometimes glows with an inner blue light.
5689. A strange ivory-graven rattle-drum shaped like an hourglass, whose discordant music is rumored to bring madness and death, when used during rites of dark magic.
5690. A strange looking stone made of a material that looks like basalt, only with small insets of strange red, almost gleaming, material. It is slightly warm to the touch, and if one examines it very carefully or is very tactile to the touch, it seems to be almost pulsating.
5691. A strange note, written on bloody human flesh. Examining the ragged piece of flesh reveals a reeking stench of sweat and tears. A series of crude gouges in the skin pulsate and seep blood. They seem to form a pattern, and the reader can just make out the following: “Beware the Avatar of the Crawling Chaos, the Heart of Darkness knows no mercy.”
5692. A strange pin, like a clothier might use for sewing, but larger, and with a slightly serrated shaft and a red glass bead for a head. Larger than is useful by a factor of two. If stuck in red-blooded flesh, into which it sinks readily, the red bead glows, faintly, flickering like a faint flame in a bloody shade.
5693. A strange rock hammer with a steelmark of Abyssal runes. The runes read “HAIL TO THE KING OF THE ROCK”
5694. A strange sextant crafted from tarnished brass, badly tanned leather, crudely shaped driftwood and milky glass, it’s a tool only a seasoned mariner could appreciate, much less love. And yet, over the years various sailors have added their own touches such as a bundle of pigeon feathers tied with sinew to a thumbscrew, a rough etching of a stylized Kraken and an extra mirror. Regardless of its dubious decorations, the object works perfectly well as a navigational tool.
5695. A strange wand that is visually translucent, but when held closely, one can hear the sound of a light spring rain.
5696. A strange, tiny inkpot, barely large enough to contain more than a few drops of ink, with a pointed, small-diameter needle protruding from its bottom. The needle, if embedded in one’s flesh, causes the pot to well with waiting red.
5697. A strangely shaped piece of whittled driftwood with dozens of holes in it. When the correct hole is blown into, it mimics the sound of the ocean.
5698. A string necklace made up of various bits of bone and teeth, presumably trophies from an adventurer’s travels. Most are from kobolds, goblins and the like, but the centerpiece tooth appears to be from a young dragon.
5699. A string of large pearl-like stones that radiate with light and warmth when the bearer’s prayers are heard by their deity. There is no guarantee that the prayer will be answered however.
5700. A stub of paper with writing on it that says something different to everyone who looks at it
5701. A stuffed and preserved head of a gnoll.
5702. A stuffed cockatrice clutching a sculpted marble hand in one talon.
5703. A stuffed jackdaw that occasionally blinks or cocks its head.
5704. A stuffed toy frog with amber, glass eyes. When in possession of the bearer, they will notice that the air around them is oddly absent of bugs.
5705. A stunning broach, embedded with a misty quartz crystal.
5706. A sturdy backpack with numerous pockets for storing items that might be needed while adventuring. Hooks are included for attaching items such as canteens, pouches, or even a rolled-up blanket. It has padded bands that strap across the chest and the waist to distribute its weight more evenly.
5707. A sturdy brass compass, painted forest green and blood red and etched with images of wolves hunting their prey.
5708. A sturdy brass compass, painted ocean blue and blood red and etched with images sharks chasing their prey.
5709. A sturdy brass compass, painted plains tan and blood red and etched with images of lions stalking their prey.
5710. A sturdy brass scroll case
5711. A sturdy ceramic jar with a heavy lid and a few tiny holes to let air in. It is half-filled with water and contains four adult leeches, each roughly six inches long. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that leeches are sometimes used for medicinal purposes in order to drain poisons or to stimulate blood flow to areas of the body, such as in cases of frostbite or large stitched together wounds. A leech can survive for six months between feedings.
5712. A sturdy cloth backpack made of high quality cotton, adorned with exotic feathers and pretty cross stitches.
5713. A sturdy iron banded wooden travel case. Inside the case are two cushions, an amount of velvet, and several silk straps all meant to secure and protect a single object; a small unlabeled vial made of thick hardened glass with a stopper that’s been covered in several layers of sealing wax. Inside the vial floats the blood and small chunks of brain matter harvested from a pig. The vial has a preserving enchantment on it keeping the blood and brain matter as fresh as if it was still alive. Suspicious PC’s may deduce that this is some sort of decoy or double of another similar case meant to serve as a distraction in order for the true prize to reach its destination.
5714. A sturdy leather cord tied into an intricate knot that writhes in the hands of the bearer.
5715. A sturdy leather wallet branded with the symbol of a pair of spears crossing over a shield. It contains a full set of certified identification papers denoting that the bearer is a corporal with a good service record in a well respected mercenary company. The section containing the member’s physical description (Height, weight, sex, race, eye, skin and hair colour) is completely blank and could be filled in by anyone with half decent handwriting.
5716. A sturdy steel scroll case
5717. A sturdy waterproof scroll tube containing detailed engineering blueprints for the construction of a ballista
5718. A sturdy waterproof scroll tube containing detailed engineering blueprints for the construction of a catapult
5719. A sturdy waterproof scroll tube containing detailed engineering blueprints for the construction of a siege tower
5720. A sturdy waterproof scroll tube containing detailed engineering blueprints for the construction of a trebuchet
5721. A sturdy wooden case containing eight large glass vials containing human hearts floating in a preservative fluid. Each vial contains a label with a name written on it belonging to a missing person in a nearby settlement.
5722. A sturdy wooden scroll tube containing a small collection of letters carefully folded and tucked away. The letters are from an adventurer’s loved ones, and have clearly all been read multiple times. The last page is actually a note meant for whoever finds the fallen adventurer, begging that her loved ones are informed of her fate.
5723. A sturdy wooden travel case containing a popular board game known as Roundels. It has similar elements to chess but is played on a circular board with a stylized keep. The game is abstract and is supposed to loosely simulate a siege. There is an attacking player and a defending player and each side has some unique pieces in addition to their common pieces. The etiquette of playing Roundels requires players to participate in two games, one as the attacker and the other as defender.
5724. A stylish jet black long coat with a furred neck.
5725. A stylized bead curtain that uses dozens of brightly coloured, thin wooden playing cards rather than beads.
5726. A suggestive mermaid figurine carved from drift wood.
5727. A suggestive shaped lantern made of red tinted glass like those commonly lit outside dens of iniquity
5728. A suitcase containing a dartboard set. It includes a multicolored board that breaks into four smaller pieces for easy travel and six brass-tipped darts. The board itself consists of a layer of of painted cork on hardwood backing.
5729. A sun token made of pure gold with dried leather headdress fittings still hanging from it.
5730. A suncatcher in the shape of an evil deity literally catches the light of the sun, forming a hazy space of shadows around it.
5731. A surgeon’s amputation saw with a bone handle engraved with pictographs of burial rituals.
5732. A surprisingly clean pair of steel needle-nose pliers
5733. A surprisingly light, cobalt blue, metal cube the length of a hand. The many holes on its surface appear to resemble stars in the night sky.
5734. A surprisingly light, obsidian coin of unknown origin.
5735. A surrealistic painting of an irresponsible sage with the head of a manta ray, dismembering an owl with whiskers like a cat in a dense jungle.
5736. A suspiciously clean wallet made from sewn mice and rats.
5737. A swanky leather dice cup.
5738. A swirling purple gem that if pressed against the forehead and held there for a few seconds, will then float in front of the bearer’s forehead until he is slain or removes it. While the gems floats around the head, the bearer feels a sense of clarity and foresight.
5739. A swirly mahogany wand that changes to a different color every night at midnight.
5740. A tabard of obvious military design from an army that no one is able to identify
5741. A tablet of fired clay. Dozens of names are written on its surface.
5742. A talisman carved from jet, in the shape of a panther’s claw.
5743. A talisman depicting a forgotten deity from a bygone era. If you held to the ear, the bearer can hear faint murmurings.
5744. A tall banner pole emblazoned with the holy symbol of the Goddess of Spiders. Adorned in skulls and the webbed and dried husks of many offerings, it oozes with an evil aura. From out of the great webbed void, a multiple-eyed arachnid gaze, looks down upon the battlefield, with slaughter reflected in its blackness. When the wind moves the tattered banner, it flutters soundlessly, like so much webbing. Yet, ever so faintly, the furtive noise of scuttling can be heard, or perhaps the clicking and chittering of steel-hard mandibles.
5745. A tall brass rod is etched with an abstract circular design that seems to be devoid of any pattern.
5746. A tall pole bearing a tattered banner consisting of crude stitching and daubed tribal markings. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify it as being orcish in origin and echoes of their bloodlust and aggression remain bound to its fabric.
5747. A tall thick candle whose tallow and wick seem to be made from pure obsidian. The candle appears to be half burned down judging by the amount of melted obsidian “wax” on the base and sides. It does not light by any normal means, but you feel as though a sufficiently powerful magic could command it to burn once more.
5748. A tall winged banner pole with hollowed-bone decorations that emit shrill, eerie shrieks when wind passes through them.
5749. A tall, black hat of a witchfinder, inside the hat is sewn the name Erasmus Pottingley.
5750. A tall, steel, teapot-like device that contains a small chamber for coffee grounds and a large chamber for water, connected by a small tube. Heating the pot forces boiling water through the tube and into the grounds. A glass knob at the top of the tube allows the user to see the color of the brew and stop when it is sufficiently strong. It can brew up to four cups of coffee at a time. It can also be used to make tea, steep medicinal herbs, or just boil water.
5751. A tapestry depicting a mythological scene.
5752. A tarnished brass kazoo in the shape of a fish.
5753. A tarnished bronze coin about the size of a palm. Mossy and damaged, this ancient coin is barely perceptible as valuable.
5754. A tarnished copper signal on a leather thong. The whistle’s mouthpiece is shaped liked a pair of pouting lips.
5755. A tarnished gold anklet chain with three tarnished charms: a windmill, a boot and a torch. A fourth, gleaming and untarnished charm, is also attached: a sailing ship.
5756. A tarnished old dog whistle. It can only be heard by mad or rabid dogs.
5757. A tarnished silver bell lacking a clapper. The worn initial H.P.L are barely visible on its lip.
5758. A tarnished silver holy symbol of the God of Secrets. It seems to twist and lean towards anyone sharing a secret or uttering a closely guarded piece of information
5759. A tattered flag with the symbol of a bloody Random Melee Weapon stitched on it.
5760. A tattered page torn from a larger manuscript that seems to portray an ancient battle and the rise of a king.
5761. A tattered remnant of a sermon written on vellum. A certain passage reads “With the certainty of stone, we shall persevere. Each crack, each mark is not a blemish, but a testament—a history of defiance writ upon our flesh?”
5762. A tattered scroll containing a dozen sentences translated from common into another language (DM’s choice), with phonetic spelling for the latter. “Surrender or die!”, “Give me all your gold!”and “Drop your weapons!” top the list, with the rest following the common theme. Some of the phrases are translated badly and are not very intimidating
5763. A tattered, oft-folded letter on which are written a mother’s pleas for her daughter to stop her dangerous adventuring and come home while they both still live.
5764. A taut black headband, clipped with a black metal skull, that can be attached on the outside of a hat or worn as a normal headband.
5765. A taxidermy wolpertinger (A hybrid of rabbit, bird, squirrel, and deer) native to Mooncalf Valley.
5766. A teak camphor chest with grinning, demented angelic handles.
5767. A teal masquerade mask crafted of leather and peacock feathers.
5768. A tear stained map of the local cemetery with an “X” marking a specific grave.
5769. A teardrop-shaped crystal that always leans to the south
5770. A teardrop-shaped crystal that forms salt crystals along the bottom surface
5771. A teardrop-shaped glass vial containing a viscous blue liquid. Looking directly at it for extended periods evokes a mild feeling of sorrow in the viewer.
5772. A telescope constructed of jade and banded with bloodstone. A creature looking through the telescope sees not the world around them, but a strange alien vista which follows the motions of the telescope.
5773. A telescopic horn, constructed of thin sheets of tin rolled into concentric cones, and measures about three feet long when fully extended. The collapsible trumpet distorts and echoes the voice and origin location of the creature using it. These items are primarily used by fraudulent mediums to disguise the true source of mysterious voices in darkened séance chambers.
5774. A ten foot length of hemp rope that can be made to stand vertically, but cannot be climbed
5775. A ten foot length of standard iron chain
5776. A ten foot long coil of rope spun from the black hair of an elven maid.
5777. A terracotta lamp with silver filigree work depicting lions killing escaping slaves.
5778. A thermometer containing a thick red liquid that looks suspiciously like fresh blood. The level of the liquid moves up or down depending on the current danger. The object is rarely reliable as it seems to have strange notions of what is and is not dangerous to humans.
5779. A thick black cowl that has the smell of damp earth lingering about it.
5780. A thick braid of dark mermaid hair entwined with kelp and sea shells that regularly drips salt water.
5781. A thick burlap sack containing four copper ingots, each weighing one pound.
5782. A thick burlap sack containing four iron ingots, each weighing one pound.
5783. A thick candle that when lit, fills the air with the smell of hot bacon and eggs
5784. A thick canvas messenger bag with the image of an anvil on one side, surrounded by four arms, each wielding a different tool.
5785. A thick caribou skin frontier jacket covered in mythological scenes.
5786. A thick cotton pouch containing a powerful lodestone (Magnet). If the lodestone is within two feet of a compass, it produces false readings.
5787. A thick crystal sphere, large as a grapefruit. Delicate fronds of green flora lie within, all sprouted in a choked abundance from a mess of roots and humus. Yellow dust, perhaps pollen, swirls about the stems amidst motes of white gas. The sphere does not open, and the plants within are like none seen on the earth.
5788. A thick glass orb containing a tablespoon of quicksilver.
5789. A thick hemp rope that ties itself into a hangman’s knot whenever it’s left unsupervised.
5790. A thick iron ring with eight chains attached. Each quarter-meter chain, composed of nine links, has a short spine protruding on either side of each link. Investigation shows that the large, central ring, which has a spike on one end, has a broken nub opposite it, where the whole affair might have been attached to a haft.
5791. A thick iron tomb plaque that has rusted away to reveal a hollow space within, in which is a rolled scroll of thin beaten gold. The scroll is etched in an ancient language (Which if translated reads: “The treasure of the Wavestar is secured in the cave of the cove.”), and has a crude map showing a coastline with unknown markings (Which can be deciphered as star readings for ships). A knowledgeable PCs can translate the language, understand the star readings and will remember that the Wavestar was a merchant ship that was presumably lost at sea over a hundred years ago.
5792. A thick pair of six-toed socks. When the bearer walks on wood, each step sounds like a hoof.
5793. A thick piece of leather on which was branded a prayer of contrition. It says that it is not enough to ask for absolution, penitence must be forced upon the impure. Some sins can only be forgiven with consecrated flame.
5794. A thick tapestry depicting angels being slain by a rampaging demon wielding a huge sword wreathed in dark energy. The tapestry is obviously old, but is in good condition.
5795. A thick treant root with glowing green veins.
5796. A thick wreath of evergreen boughs decorated with a bright red ribbon. The boughs have been magically preserved and will never wilt, fade or lose their scent.
5797. A thick, charcoal-colored infinity scarf smelling of wood smoke and large enough to act as a deep hood as well as scarf.
5798. A thick, white rope tied in a circle to be worn as a collar. If the bearer closes their eyes and smells the rope, they catch the scent of a far off ocean breeze. No one except for the bearer experiences this smell.
5799. A thick, wide leather belt that has a buckle shaped like a fist and various attachment points from which to hang pouches and perhaps even a small sack or two. A hidden compartment lurks at the back of the belt.
5800. A thimble on which is an enamel painting of a turtle
5801. A thin chisel wrought in the shape of a stylized finger, with a perpetually flaking lacquer of dark green.
5802. A thin length of rope with many oddly shaped bits of hollow metal fixed along its length. Commonly known as a roar cord, a creature can swing it over their head to generate a variety of eerie noises.
5803. A thin malachite bracelet with a silver clasp.
5804. A thin metal rod that plays out a simple tune when repeatedly tapped
5805. A thin metal template with the complete alphabet (In common) and numbers 0 to 9 for stenciling.
5806. A thin steel sign, three feet wide by two feet tall, that in bold red writing states “Beware of Dog”.
5807. A thin wooden case containing a dozen, foot-long sticks of incense wrapped in waxed paper.
5808. A thin wooden sign, two feet wide by one foot tall, that in bold red writing states “Beware of the Leopard”.
5809. A thin, black leather cord with a single small red bead threaded onto it. The bead is freezing cold to touch but causes no harm to the bearer.
5810. A thin, lily-white amphora, this vessel is without adornment of any kind except for a single hairline crack down its body. Twelve inches from top to bottom and four inches across at the shoulder, it is small as amphorae go.
5811. A thin, rectangular wooden board, printed with an arching array of alphabetical letters, numbers, and short affirmative and negative messages. Paired with a small, heart-shaped pointer on short wooden legs, this device enables communication with spiritual entities or the user’s deep subconscious mind. This tool (Known as a ouija or talking board) is commonly used by mediums during séances when trying to communicate with the dead.
5812. A thin, square foot of iron with a hole in the centre and unknown text engraved on both sides.
5813. A three fingered glove that makes everything taste like burning
5814. A three fingered glove that sounds like women laughing in the far distance when near corpses
5815. A three foot length of steel chain that glows blue if touched by sweat or salt water
5816. A three foot long tusk of a great beast, fashioned into a spiralled horn that produces an odd piping sound when blown.
5817. A three foot tall chalice carved in the shape of an open mawed dragon resting on its curled tail. It is made from petrified dragon bone of mottled purple and blood red hues. It is worked with sharp, horny and scaly looking protrusions that one can quite easily cut themselves on.
5818. A three piece cutlery set made out of driftwood. The knife is set with a clam shell blade and is surprisingly sharp.
5819. A three-dimensional puzzle in the form of a drinking vessel commonly known as a fuddling cup. It is made of three cups all linked together by holes and tubes. The challenge of the puzzle is to drink from the vessel in such a way that the beverage does not spill. To do this successfully, the cups must be drunk from in a specific order. This sort of puzzle is commonly found at taverns as drinking in the incorrect order will result in the drink spilling on the user.
5820. A three-inch tall, carved, pine figurine of a comical looking butler. The carving doesn’t move, but every once and a while, its owner can see the figurine winking out of the corner of his eye.
5821. A three-lobed spinning device with almost frictionless ball bearings in the center. There are holes in the lobes and the center has a raised disk on both sides so the device can be held while it is spinning.
5822. A thumb sized spherical glass globe that accurately displays the planet. The land is painted gold and the water is painted silver.
5823. A thumb sized, winged pig figurine made of black marble.
5824. A thumb-sized bronze horned owl. When the head is turned clockwise three full rotations, it flaps its wings and makes hooting noises as the head rotates back.
5825. A ticket to a dwarvern opera, to be performed a tenday hence
5826. A tightly rolled cloth that unfurls into a solid black banner, with a faint, hard-to-look-at pattern shimmering in the weave.
5827. A tightly rolled scroll. Reading reveals it to be someone’s diary. They apparently had a dramatic life.
5828. A tightly rolled vellum scroll, apparently blank on both sides, but with a deep blue shimmer as it catches the light.
5829. A timber walking cane, polished to a mirror shine
5830. A tin box decorated with an embossing of a ship in a bottle, containing precision woodworking and knot tying tools with telescoping handles.
5831. A tin bucket without a label, filled with what appears to be loose teeth. A closer look confirms the contents: dozens of molars, bicuspids, incisors, canines. Human teeth. Enough to fill at least five or six mouths. Resting on top of the pile is a rusted pair of iron pliers.
5832. A tin case containing a dozen small purple sugar balls that taste like the hottest and sweetest bit of delight you’ve ever had.
5833. A tin dog whistle shaped like a howling wolf.
5834. A tin of purple powder, when the powder comes into contact with blood it glows for ten minutes.
5835. A tin plated halfling skull.
5836. A tin whistle on a chain, with a single goose feather attached to it.
5837. A tiny bat skin pouch containing a pinch of lich dust..
5838. A tiny box with a button that makes a faint chiming noise when pressed
5839. A tiny bronze idol of a goblin carrying a knife in both hands and one knife clutched in his teeth.
5840. A tiny bronze kettle, or perhaps a retort, dark with tarnish. It has a curling chimney, rather than a spout. When liquid is heated inside, it immediately evaporates.
5841. A tiny cage in which different things appear, sometimes living, sometimes not. There appears to be no way to open or break the cage.
5842. A tiny cage with no door
5843. A tiny chest carved to look like it has numerous feet on the bottom
5844. A tiny dead sparrow, wrapped delicately in giant green leaves.
5845. A tiny doll depicting a blonde, blue eyes child, when placed down it’s eyes appear to follow people.
5846. A tiny doll made out of straw, when not observed, it’s clothes change to match those of the person possessing it.
5847. A tiny finch made of overlapping metal plates. A gentle tug on its tail causes it to unfold into the shape of a small flower. Touching the centre of the flower causes it to reconfigure into the shape of a small lizard.
5848. A tiny glass vial with silver end-caps and a chain for wearing as an amulet. Every full moon a message printed on a slip of rolled paper appears inside.
5849. A tiny gnome-crafted music box that plays a song you dimly remember from your childhood
5850. A tiny green ingot, pale emerald all the way through, as verdigris, in a torn-open lead envelope. On contact with skin, it turns the flesh a spongy, pale green, causes chills, and elicits an itching from the scalp. A geometric, faceted signet has been pressed into one corner of the ingot, presumably while it was cast.
5851. A tiny hand carved from amber
5852. A tiny harp with strings made of glowing energy
5853. A tiny jade figurine of a praying mantis. Staring at it for one minute causes horrifying visions of an impending disaster.
5854. A tiny lidded box that opens, revealing the emptiness of space. No amount of force can make an object go into the box.
5855. A tiny lizard skull that has been fashioned into a shot glass.
5856. A tiny longsword (No longer than a pinky finger) made from magnetic iron.
5857. A tiny mechanical crab or spider that moves about when it’s not being observed
5858. A tiny mechanical dragonfly with a miniature glass vial at its center
5859. A tiny metal mouth that spits out words all day long, at random
5860. A tiny metal shard that floats on water.
5861. A tiny metallic box without latches or locks that only opens on a full moon
5862. A tiny model ship built inside an empty potion bottle.
5863. A tiny opal jar filled with extremely fine, bright blue dust.
5864. A tiny orb of an unknown metallic alloy
5865. A tiny porcelain doll with unnervingly human eyes.
5866. A tiny rabbit skull that whispers scathing insults when nobody is at it.
5867. A tiny sailing ship made of fine crystal inside a bottle with no clear way it got in there. It shimmers in the sunlight and radiates mysterious magic.
5868. A tiny silver bell without a clapper
5869. A tiny silver icon of a raven
5870. A tiny sketch portrait of a goblin
5871. A tiny skull themed toy castle with a hinged “jawbridge” and a handful of tin soldiers.
5872. A tiny soapstone octopus that slowly absorbs water into itself at a rate of about a single drop per hour. No matter how much it absorbs, the figurine never gets any heavier and for all intents and purposes, the water completely disappears.
5873. A tiny statuette of an emaciated, skeletal man that occasionally winks.
5874. A tiny stone orb that hangs in the air for a moment when thrown
5875. A tiny wooden horse with white hair for a mane and tail, and silver beads for eyes.
5876. A tiny, glass, thin necked bottle, stoppered with a cork containing the smallest denomination of the local currency inside it. The penny is much large than the opening of the bottle and cannot be removed. Knowledgeable PC’s will suspect that the glass was blown around the penny and that this is likely a souvenir for travelers from afar
5877. A tiny, polished wooden coffin. The lid is sealed with wax and the sound of tumbling glass shards can be heard as the box is moved.
5878. A tiny, stoppered vial made of what appears to be crystal that contains a dark red liquid.
5879. A titanium sewing needle that can only unweave thread.
5880. A tool designed to crack nuts. It disintegrates shells, leaving the nuts untouched. Bloody marks between the teeth and weird stains on the handle leave disturbing thoughts as to what it has been used for recently.
5881. A tooled leather bookmark that was used in the library of the God of Knowledge.
5882. A tooth from an unknown beast
5883. A torc fashioned from a coiled length of interlocking chains, constructed of two unknown metals. Black and white, they swirl around each other like yin and yang, darkness and light.
5884. A torn and filthy burlap sack. Inside are four holy symbols each dedicated to a different minor God each from a Random Good Domain. Each has been defaced. A fifth holy symbol (Also dedicated to a minor God of a Random Good Domain) has not yet been vandalized.
5885. A torn and frayed foot long scarlet ribbon that smells and feels like your mother’s skin. It’s comforting and causes you to feel oddly guilty that you haven’t spoken to her in awhile.
5886. A tortoise shell table snuff-box set with six crow feet.
5887. A tortoise shell the size of a grown man’s torso.
5888. A toy crossbow that shoots small blunt bolts
5889. A toy horse carved from bone.
5890. A toy sword that whistles a children’s song when swung.
5891. A translucent and oddly shaped prism that seems to fade in and out of existence when seen in daylight.
5892. A translucent coin, minted in an unknown land
5893. A translucent conch shell that becomes opaque when submerged in saltwater.
5894. A translucent green stone the size of a fat grape. The item is sea glass, a fragment of a bottle that washed around the world and back, until it had no sharp edges.
5895. A translucent pearl with a coral blue shimmer.
5896. A transparent fist sized cube containing an hourglass-shaped vial. A figure is inside the hourglass on one side. When the cube is tilted, the figure disappears, and a different figure appears on the other side. Depending on how the cube is tilted, different figures may appear.
5897. A transparent glass polyhedron about the size of a human fist. Light passing through it tends to blend into white, and to magnify, so that when in the open it glows with pure white bubbles of light.
5898. A transparent glass sphere encloses a miniature scene of a river of tar running through a wasteland. It also contains a mystical blue liquid, which seems like it would mix with the tar if the globe were shaken. Perceptive PC’s will notice ancient, indecipherable writing around the outer base.
5899. A travel accordion or squeezebox covered in small nicks and patterns of paints that were once brightly colored
5900. A travel case filled with strange, forbidden, and in some cases heretical, religious scrolls. Neither the case nor the papers bear any sort of marker’s mark, ownership or distinguishing names.
5901. A travel case for a Random Musical Instrument. The case is made of hardwood covered in boiled leather sealed in beeswax with rubber seals around the opening. The inside is lined with velvet sheltering the instrument from the harshest jostling the bearer might endure. An adjustable carry strap allows the bearer to wear it in whatever manner is most comfortable for them. Whatever type of instrument the case is deigned to carry, the case contains one such musical device (Or a set of devices) within it.
5902. A travel pouch made of an elephant’s ear.
5903. A travel set of a dozen paints. All of the black paint is used up
5904. A travel trunk of shoemaker’s tools in serviceable condition.
5905. A traveler’s scroll case containing ten small sheets of vellum.
5906. A tree branch bearing an odd symbol that looks like it was grown rather than carved.
5907. A triangular shaped, silk battle pennant, displaying a black dragon on a red field.
5908. A tribal battle mask made from wood, bone and dark paints that covers the bearer’s face and presents a hateful, leering countenance. The mask show’s excellent craftsmanship and sports exquisite details.
5909. A trio of bone dice with silver-filled pips
5910. A trio of cloth arm bands. One labelled “Happy”, one labelled “Sad”, and one labelled “Orange”.
5911. A tube of bamboo has been carefully sealed at both ends with wax. Scratched into the side are the words “Miskin’s Stash”. The tube contains a pair of clean, dry socks and underwear.
5912. A tube of lip gloss that makes the user’s lips invisible for one hour when applied.
5913. A tubular instrument that gradually broadens towards the lower end. It is made out of wood, with a double reed at one end and a metal or wooden flared bell at the other end. Known as a shehnai, its sound is thought to create and maintain a sense of auspiciousness and sanctity and, as a result, is sometimes used during marriages, processions and in temples although it is also played in concerts.
5914. A tuning fork that emits a chord when struck
5915. A turquoise courtier’s uniform adorned with the stylized symbol of a dagger poised above a cup just above the bearer’s heart. Crow’s feathers dangle from the epaulettes.
5916. A tweed flatcap that never gets wet.
5917. A twisted sacred icon of black metal, scorched and pitted as if by the heat of some great forge or furnace. Arcane symbols of power are etched into its surface, writhing around each other and intertwining as if alive. At the icon’s apex is a single crystal eye, whose cold gaze constantly searches the area for enemies.
5918. A twisted warhorn blasted into a dark ebony hue and wrapped in bands of bronze with draconic runes that glow with purple eldritch fire. The low moaning drone of the horn discomforts all who hear it.
5919. A twisted, grey wooden walking cane sized for a halfling.
5920. A twisting brass horn that produces a haunting melody when blow.
5921. A two inch disk of ebony. One side of the disk is inscribed with three intertwined serpents. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize the ebony tri-serpent is a symbol of the Six Eyes, a network of informants that sell their services to the highest bidder.
5922. A two pronged tuning fork made of steel
5923. A two pronged tuning fork made of steel that’s constantly humming in F
5924. A two pronged tuning fork made of steel that’s constantly humming in F unless actually exposed to an F tone at which point it becomes silent.
5925. A two-faced silver coin with the image of a fox. When it is flipped, the face of the creature who flipped it appears in place of the fox for a minute before returning to normal.
5926. A two-foot length of catgut rope with knots tied every four inches.
5927. A two-foot tube of rolled paper.
5928. A two-foot-long wooden rod bearing a silver wolf’s-head at the tip, and its length is inscribed with moon-sigils.
5929. A two-gallon cask containing a peck of pickled peppers.
5930. A type of novelty lamp that was popular among some social circles a few decades ago. It consists of a wax mixture of a deep red color inside a conical glass vessel, the remainder of which contains a light blue translucent liquid. The vessel rests on a black iron frame with the very base decorated as a beach scene. The frame containing a candle whose heat and light are channeled into the bottom of the glass where the wax rests. The warmed wax rises through the surrounding liquid, cools, loses its buoyancy, and falls back to the bottom of the vessel in a cycle. While the lamp is lit the entire structure bears a striking resemblance to an active volcano.
5931. A uniform belonging to the nearby town guard or local militia. It is however lacking the appropriate weapons and armor.
5932. A used and bloodstained crossbow bolt, encased in a glass rectangle. It’s probably a war trophy of some sort and realistically should be mounted on some sort of plaque denoting the bolt’s significance.
5933. A used branding-iron bearing the sigil of the Manticora Trading House.
5934. A used hangman’s noose that feels heavier than it has a right to be.
5935. A used hangman’s noose that fills whoever holds it with a sense that justice was done
5936. A used incense burner crafted from a human sternum.
5937. A vampire fang capped with gold.
5938. A vampire fang carved into the bust of a beautiful woman.
5939. A varnished case containing a string of garlic, two wooden stakes and a silver cross.
5940. A varnished orb, eight inches in diameter, made from a nothic’s eye.
5941. A velvet cloth that erases most scratches, scuffs, and ink when passed over them.
5942. A velvet coin purse containing half a dozen egg-sized polished stones in various natural hues.
5943. A velvet pouch containing a small silver bell, a razor blade, and a sprig of holly
5944. A velvet pouch containing an ornate set of playing cards
5945. A velvet pouch filled with coarse sand that feels weightless.
5946. A venomous scorpion encased in a clear glass prism.
5947. A ventriloquist’s dummy, made of finely sculpted and painted wood. It is shaped and dressed like a successful merchant and roughly two feet tall. An insert in the back of the dummy allows creature to manipulate it with one arm.
5948. A very small paper box which contains a tiny twig that disappears in a puff of smoke when taken out of the box, only to reappear in the box 24 hours later.
5949. A vest fashioned from the hide of a large darkhaired ape.
5950. A vest with one hundred tiny pockets
5951. A vial of dragon blood
5952. A vial of oil labelled “Tomonari’s anointment for long swords and other weapons of the distinguished nobleman.”
5953. A vial of scented oil that can be burned as incense of worn as a perfume. Everyone smells something different but, always something nostalgic and a bit sad.
5954. A vibrant, blue jay feather that never frays or bends.
5955. A vibrantly coloured mask, made from the feathers of a variety of rare and beautiful songbirds.
5956. A vicious looking carved ivory talon. Creatures holding the talon yearn to hunt and grasp prey in their hands and rend their weak flesh to pieces.
5957. A violet satin facemask with purple silk ties.
5958. A violet shawl that twists and melds with the darkness, becoming as black as coal in even the faintest shadow.
5959. A violin case containing a functioning violin and bow, sized for a cat sized creature
5960. A violin case containing a functioning violin and bow, sized for a mouse sized creature
5961. A vitrified eye of a beholder that blinks and looks around of its own accord. The word Beauty” has been carved into it’s pupil.
5962. A vivid, deep purple crystal that appears to consist of a random assortment of thousands of tiny cubes bound together. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as fluorite.
5963. A voodoo doll that resembles a prominent individual that recently died in the local area. The doll has a bloodstone tucked inside of it and is covered in dried human blood.
5964. A votive candle holder in the shape of a lighthouse.
5965. A vulture beak with string tied to it to make a mask
5966. A walking cane made from an exotic mummified snake.
5967. A walking cane made of whitish bamboo that looks like fossilized bone. The cane is topped with a large oval amber that has a mosquito trapped in its center.
5968. A walking cane with an iron ferule that strikes sparks on stone
5969. A walking stick, shod with silver and with the head of an eagle as its handle. The eagle’s head once had two garnets for eyes but these have long since become mislaid. If the head is grasped firmly and unscrewed, it will reveal a long but thin container which can be used to store liquor.
5970. A walnut, iron, and onyx pipe with a clay bowl depicting a swan.
5971. A walrus shaped fetish, made from the tusk of the animal that it resembles.
5972. A wand crafted by moon druids in honour of the great beasts of nature. The core is made of a dryad sapling grown from the fertile remains of a large beast. The bones grow around the sprout and meld into it.
5973. A wand made from a rare elm with grains of sand sprinkled across its handle.
5974. A wand made of a line of conjoined tiny rodent skulls with emerald eyes that makes it a grisly site to behold.
5975. A wand made of a sturdy ash. Each end is seamlessly reinforced with bronze.
5976. A wanted poster that bears the face of one of the PC’s but has the name Clay Allison written as name of the outlaw. The bounty can be collected “Dead or Alive” and the reward is substantial.
5977. A wanted poster that resembles one of the PC’s but the hairstyle and colour are completely wrong.
5978. A war banner depicting a bone-white skeleton on a field of midnight blue.
5979. A war banner that’s shredded, torn, and stained with blood, this standard has seen more than a single battle. The image of a red maw devouring sacred flames stands atop a field of black.
5980. A war medal given to those who fought in the Goblin Wars over a hundred years ago.
5981. A warm glowing pebble emitting a beautiful phasing aura of seven colors, with a very rare eight.
5982. A warm, black iron marble; like a very large bearing, but rougher. The rough, iron exterior has worn away at a thin spot, showing a smooth, shiny yolk of hard, tungsten-grey metal within. Balmy warmth emanates from the marble, but slowly burns nearby skin an irritated red, if kept nearby for too long. The redness takes time to fade, and flakes and weeps before it does.
5983. A water-skin that sounds like it has something thicker than water in it.
5984. A waterproof belt pouch containing five silk packets containing powdered illuminators pigments. When thrown into a fire they ignite, causing the flames to burn a Random Bright Color for a few seconds.
5985. A waterproof drawstring sack containing a dozen hard-packed soap mixtures, commonly known as bath bombs. The soaps are shaped and decorated to look like large strawberries and when submerged in water, they begin foaming excitedly putting off an impressive amount of bubbles and the scent of strawberries. Knowledgeable PC’s know that these luxuries are often used in the private baths of nobles and the elite as well as high end bathhouses.
5986. A waterproof leather pouch containing several dozen compressed fuel pellets. These small objects are made from compacted sawdust and are typically used by blacksmiths and alchemists to better regulate their furnaces and burners. Since the pellets are of a uniform size, weight, density and material they burn at an easily measurable temperature for a certain amount of time. These pellets could be used in small travel burners or as kindling for campfires.
5987. A waterproof satchel containing a sea blue masquerade mask, with a slim wooden handle.
5988. A waterproof scrollcase containing a key and a sheaf of official documents. According to the paperwork, the key opens a bankbox in a prestigious bank in a large city far away, and the lease on it has been paid for the next 100 years.
5989. A waterproof scrollcase filled with parchments covered in artificer’s diagrams for various inventions, mainly those relating to what looks like attempts to create flying machines. The case contains a fair bit of sawdust, metal shavings and other evidence that the arcane engineer may have already started construction.
5990. A waterproof wax paper envelope containing a careworn letter from a dying father addressed to his daughter asking for forgiveness
5991. A wax paper packet filled with dried rose petals.
5992. A wax seal stamp with the design of a crown on it.
5993. A wax stamp that changes insignia depending on the proximity of the nearest royal blooded individual.
5994. A waxed scroll on which is written a complex alchemical formula. The recipe is not titled and seems to be for some sort of explosive but an knowledgeable PC can determine that it’s actually instructions for making soap.
5995. A wearable shoulder harness, designed to hold a lantern above and behind the bearer, leaving their hands free.
5996. A weathered, hand sized, stone figurine of a woman, seemingly unclothed but wrapped in hair that falls to her ankles.
5997. A weathered, stained, dented but still sealed scroll case containing various battle plans from an ancient conflict known as the War of Three Kingdoms. These plans were written by the master strategist Moru and were believed to have been lost long ago in transit to or from the front lines.
5998. A wedding cake topper depicting a young elf woman dressed in leathers and a human man in wizard robes.
5999. A wedge of glass that shows the image of three strangely garbed children playing and one frightened child watching you
6000. A weighted copper coin that, when flipped, always lands on its edge.
6001. A weighted trick con that always lands on the same side no matter how many times its flipped.
6002. A well crafted iron smith’s hammer, crafted from a single piece of fine steel. The hammer’s head bears the design of two fists coming together with an anvil between them.
6003. A well crafted stylus of unfamiliar design. The user always finds themselves writing in an unknown language while using it. If they attempt to draw a map with the stylus, the user instead draws a map to a distant realm, perhaps the home of the language.
6004. A well crafted, black cloth banner featuring a stylized skull breathing flames. A Knowledgeable PC will recognize the object serves as a rallying point for the free company of mercenaries known as the Black Company, who are highly respect and feared by allies and foes alike.
6005. A well detailed tentacle made of copper.
6006. A well made bracelet of silver chain with small silver heart charms hanging off each link. A single one of these charms is carved from a rose zircon, which gives off a small amount of heat.
6007. A well made but scaled down fishing rod, one foot in length. When dipped in the water, it expands to a full six feet in length for eight hours.
6008. A well made saddle sized for a very small animal, such as a squirrel or a small cat
6009. A well made violin that makes no sound when played.
6010. A well oiled and preserved, pelt of a child werewolf.
6011. A well taxidermied fox, mounted on an oak plaque covered in green felt.
6012. A well-crafted marble chess board with silver pieces as white and gold pieces as black. Closer examination reveals that the pieces are simply silver leaf over nickel and gold leaf over brass.
6013. A well-loved teddy bear missing one of its button eyes. An observer who looks at feels a strong urge to comfort the bear, wanting to repair it. Yet for some reason they wish to repair it with an actual fresh humanoid eye.
6014. A well-made backpack that appears to be well used, and quite ordinary. It is constructed of finely tanned leather, and the straps have brass hardware and buckles. It has two side pouches, each of which appears large enough to hold about a quart of material.
6015. A well-made leather satchel with a single rune emblazoned on it that contains hair of every length and colour, from cirrus-coloured wisps of septuagenarian hair, to the most luxuriant and lengthy hair fibers of an elven lad. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize the rune as one belonging to an offshoot religious group of a popular god of strength who believe that a man’s strength is held in his hair. This satchel contains offerings of hair collected from men who willingly sacrificed their strength to their god.
6016. A well-made paperweight cast from brass. It has a tiny illusion spell that suspends an image of a proud, three masted warship sailing above it.
6017. A well-made satchel of red-dyed leather with brass fasteners. Inside the satchel rests a variety of coded financial records that (If deciphered) detail some of the illegal workings of the Moonscar Assassins’ guild.
6018. A well-polished brass oil lamp covered with intricate characters and a strange landscape in relief.
6019. A well-worn brass locket with a small drawing of a dwarven woman inside, she has a fantastic beard.
6020. A well-worn ivory drinking horn etched with indigo leaf patterns and silver cap attached by slim yet robust chain.
6021. A werewolf’s talon, during the day it resembles a human fingernail and at night a black wolf’s claw.
6022. A whale baleen broach carved into the shape of a humpback whale breaching the water.
6023. A whale tooth decanter scrimshawed with the image of a gargoyle within a star.
6024. A wheel of fine, aged cheese, one foot in diameter and sealed in wax.
6025. A wheeled trunk containing a dozen detailed marionettes and puppet show props.
6026. A whetstone made of meteoric rock.
6027. A whetstone that will sharpen blades, but only if the bearer asks nicely first. If the bearer does not ask, every blade they attempt to sharpen will become increasingly dull.
6028. A whistle made from deer antler with a silver mouthpiece. Its single mid-range tone is strong and audible at a long distance.
6029. A whistle made from gold-colored wood
6030. A whistle made from the shell of a large mollusc.
6031. A whistle that, when blown, makes no noise but makes the bearer feel certain that there’s a horse not too far away from him
6032. A white and blue porcelain doll that seems to be the smallest from a set of nesting dolls.
6033. A white burial shroud that gives an unsettling feeling to any creatures near it
6034. A white ceramic mug with an unknown substance or creature that has excess of writhing gray tendrils coming out of it.
6035. A white ceramic theater mask shaped in a caricature of a happy smiling face.
6036. A white cloth mask decorated with patterns of light green whorls that makes the bearer’s breath smell and taste like peppermint while worn.
6037. A white domino mask that covers the area around the eyes and the space between them. It has small holes on each end, suggesting it could be held on by string or a leather thong. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that traditionally these masks are held on by spirit glue, resin or tree gum rather than strings.
6038. A white flower that always attracts bugs and never dies.
6039. A white handkerchief, slightly yellowed, bordered in black thread. The soft, silky weave sloughs away all soaked or stuck-on mess once fully dried, no matter how dirty. Impossible to tear, by hand, but frayed around the edges. Bears a monogram in one corner; a rune reminiscent of a G, but with more curls.
6040. A white leather sporran edged with black horsehair bears a rearing stallion on the front.
6041. A white leather wallet decorated with a twirling red stripe, containing a full set of certified identification papers denoting that the bearer is an established member of the barbers, surgeons and dentists guild. The section containing the member’s physical description (Height, weight, sex, race, eye, skin and hair colour) is completely blank and could be filled in by anyone with half decent handwriting.
6042. A white marble bowl with the image of an ermine, and a rearing horse as pictures in a book within a shield shape and set with an amethyst. It is of strangely organic workmanship.
6043. A white marble mortar, quite small, chained to its pestle with a thin iron leash. Stained on the interior with blotchy brown. Carved on the outside with simple images of tiny, impish individuals grinding teeth in mortars just large enough to accommodate a molar.
6044. A white porcelain mask with a large pair of eye-holes with black streaks running down to a mouth stretched into a too-wide black grin. The eye holes are filled in with an opaque black substance, yet the bearer is able to see without issue. When worn, glowing red pupils appear in the eyes, and move as if they belonged to the bearer.
6045. A white porcelain mask, smooth and beautiful, except for the tears of blood coming from the eye sockets.
6046. A white porcelain tea set decorated with blue snowflakes. The set includes a teapot, bronze brazier, and six cups.
6047. A white satin glove with no visible seams, that can be easily turned inside-out to wear on a left or right hand.
6048. A white silk wedding veil
6049. A white straightjacket fashioned of durable canvas and leather straps, this instrument of restraint prevents its wearer from using his arms entirely. Properly binding a creature in a straitjacket requires one minute, and the creature must be willing, helpless or physically forced.
6050. A white tabard bearing a golden sunburst impressed upon a crimson shepherd’s crook embroidered over where the bearer’s heart should be.
6051. A white toga, neatly folded and immaculately clean, which smells strongly of damp musty earth and is reminiscent of a grave.
6052. A white veil with a silvered chain. The silvered chain is incredibly strong, and could be used as a garrotte.
6053. A white walking cane, suggesting the previous owner was blind. The cane has the initials M.K. carved into the handle.
6054. A white, sequined glove sized for a human
6055. A wicked wand made of two withered and twisted branches, with one single leaf to the side and a small skull tied by a string at the base. The wand has a uncomfortable chill to the touch and sometimes sends shivers through the body.
6056. A wicker basket containing several loafs of weighty bread wrapped in a thin blanket. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize it as Stonebread, a type of baked good made by dwarves. Usually made into round loaves, this foodstuff is so foul-tasting that only dwarfs can eat it. In fact, it is rumored that it is made using rock dust rather than flour. However, to the dwarfs, it is hearty fare that is very fortifying. In addition, it keeps extremely well.
6057. A wicker basket with a small pair of mink furs within. The furs are musty but could be easily cleaned by a professional.
6058. A wicker doll that wards off the bad dreams of children and adolescents.
6059. A wide cap made from the preserved remains of a Flumph.
6060. A wide iron-studded dog collar.
6061. A wide steel bowl with large handles on either side. It contains moulded riffles along the interior of one side of the pan and knowledgeable PC’s will recognize that it used for panning water deposits for small amounts of gold.
6062. A wide-brimmed leather hat that has a tall crown with a crease down the middle. A trio of small, gold rings pierces the hat’s brim along one side.
6063. A wide, flat bronze bracelet carved with couples entwining.
6064. A wig made from from someone executed by beheading
6065. A willow dowser’s wand, that occasionally twitches when held.
6066. A wind chime consisting of a series of suspended animal bones of various sizes, strung on braided sinew cord. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that wind chimes are often hung outside buildings by the superstitious in order to deter spirits. These instruments can also serve as musical decoration or act as a makeshift alarm.
6067. A wind chime consisting of a series of suspended brass bells of various sizes, strung on hemp rope. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that wind chimes are often hung outside buildings by the superstitious in order to deter spirits. These instruments can also serve as musical decoration or act as a makeshift alarm.
6068. A wind chime consisting of a series of suspended steel tubes of various sizes, strung on silk rope. Knowledgeable PC’s will know that wind chimes are often hung outside buildings by the superstitious in order to deter spirits. These instruments can also serve as musical decoration or act as a makeshift alarm.
6069. A wine bottle sealed with wax containing a rolled vellum scroll.
6070. A wine case containing a bottle labelled Rowfred’s Finest Red, depicting a cheery bald fellow raising his glass with a sly wink.
6071. A wineskin that only holds wine. Any other liquid placed within, bleeds out through the leather after a few seconds.
6072. A wire sculpture of a flower that releases petals when you blow on it. The petals “grow” back eventually.
6073. A wistful portrait of a singular dark mountain on a broad plain, trees covering the top third of it and nowhere else, far higher than trees ever normally grow on a mountain. The middle of the mountain sports two large overhangs of rock above deep depressions, not quite caves. A stream flows down the bottom third of the mountain, splitting its flow around an overhang that shelters a massive cavern entrance that goes who knows how deep.
6074. A withered, black human heart that is disturbingly cool to the touch.
6075. A wizard’s wand made of a three-foot long sprig of ash with a fine, smooth handle that widens at the end.
6076. A wizened, shrunken head.
6077. A wolf statuette carved from reddish limestone that fits comfortably in one hand.
6078. A wolf-skin fur cloak that sometimes causes the bearer to growl under his breath.
6079. A wood carving of an owl, so lifelike the eyes might blink at any moment.
6080. A wood cased harmonica trimmed in tin
6081. A woodcut image of a monstrous beast celebrating its bloody victory over a peaceful race.
6082. A woodcut relief depicting a woman in a rocking chair, knitting scarves and sweaters for her many grandchildren around. A warm hearth’s fire lights the room in a golden glow, giving tone to each feature of each of the children’s smiling faces. Only something thing is off about the picture. The woman eyes are sunken in holes of what might have once been eyes, her mouth a dried picture of a smile stuck into place like the muscles seized up in a corpse, her hands covered in lumpy growths which accent her impossibly knobby fingers. And weirdly, where there might be disgust or horror, the viewer only feels sympathy like one sufferer feels towards another sufferer.
6083. A woodcutter’s axe, the head of which shimmers like downwards-flowing water.
6084. A wooden abacus with fortune telling symbols painted across its beads. It occasionally self animates and acts of its own accord, locking up for a brief moment before the beads spin wildly then stop with several symbols facing upwards before moving as normal again.
6085. A wooden and metal icosahedron (20 sided die) that measures about six inches in diameter. Each face of the crux is carved with a different rune, and when one looks upon the thing, the observer has the unsettling sensation that they can see too many or too few sides at once.
6086. A wooden armband, intricately carved with interweaving vines and snaking dragons’ heads,
6087. A wooden ball that cannot be burned, but freezing it turns it to vapor
6088. A wooden ball with a spell on it to make it extremely bouncy. Whenever it hits a surface, it makes a comical “Boing!” sound.
6089. A wooden birdhouse that randomly reproduces strange bird sounds.
6090. A wooden birdhouse, carved and painted to look like a castle.
6091. A wooden box containing a collection of cards full of monsters, spells, lands and other pictures. There are symbols on each one, and the ones that repeat depict the sun, a skull, a fire, a tree and a drop of water.
6092. A wooden box containing a dozen iron nails taken from the coffin of a murderer
6093. A wooden box containing a dozen normal looking candles. However, when lit and continuing until extinguished, these candles will give off an aroma that at first seems soothing but within a minute or two will start to irritate the eyes of all those nearby
6094. A wooden box containing a dozen thick candles made from a blend of rare wax from the giant bees of fabled Ind, mixed with the swear of scholars. When they burn, a musky odor rises from the flames.
6095. A wooden box containing a dozen white candles with black crescent moons that dot their cylindrical bodies.
6096. A wooden box containing a mummified fish with feathered wings
6097. A wooden box containing a reasonably difficult jigsaw puzzle depicting a lovely pastoral scene.
6098. A wooden box containing a set of brightly coloured candles shaped to look like numbers. There are two candles from every digit, from zero to nine.
6099. A wooden box containing a set of dominoes. There are 28 white marble tiles with pips on each end.
6100. A wooden box containing a set of toy metal soldiers, each immaculately modelled to depict the livery of some forgotten nation.
6101. A wooden box containing a severed human hand. Despite the somewhat severe issue of not being connected to a body, the hand seems to be doing pretty well for itself. There are no signs of rot or decay.
6102. A wooden box containing a sewing kit, a harmonica, and a set of dice.
6103. A wooden box containing a trapmaker’s kit, including caltrops and the materials and tools needed to make a series of tripwires, snares and jaw-traps.
6104. A wooden box containing all the tools needed to create quick drying clay plaster casts. According to the instructions on the inside of the lid, the user can mix one of the vials of plaster powder with water, set it in a footprint and allow it to fully dry, all within a five minute time span. The plaster cast of the foot / hand / paw print can then be preserved, analyzed or compared to later tracks. There is enough quick drying plaster vials to create five footprint sized casts.
6105. A wooden box decorated with the image of a stick figure man. It contains ten candles made from the fat of human corpses
6106. A wooden box which contains twelve more boxes each progressively smaller. The final box is approximately two inches in diameter and contains a miniature stoppered vial. If opened the vial lets out an acrid smelling smoke and booming laughter is heard.
6107. A wooden box with a ceramic bottom that holds a living worm with a head on each end of its body
6108. A wooden box with a switch on the outside. When the switch is flipped on, a flap in the box opens, a little arm comes out, and it pushes the switch back off, before disappearing.
6109. A wooden case containing a set of metal needles, styluses, and brands. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as a pyrography or woodburning kit and it is used to draw on leather or wood by burning it. Using the tools requires a fire source; the needle or stylus is heated in the fire and held in a thick leather handle, then used like a pen.
6110. A wooden case containing a small heart-shaped piece of wood with a hole in its center and folding spirit planchette (Also known as a ouija board) printed with each letter of the alphabet, numbers 0-9 and the words “YES” and “NO”.
6111. A wooden case containing an ornate chess set. The board itself depicts the hellish Abyss and the tortured souls trapped there. The pieces on one side are various species of demons, while various types of devils making up the opposing side. It seems to be a reference to the Blood War between demons and devils.
6112. A wooden case containing an ornate chessboard. Half the board is white and heavenly with various species of celestial angels as pieces and half of which is hellish and fiery, with nightmarish demons as pieces.
6113. A wooden case containing n ancient, slender spearhead that is decorated with whirls and whorls engraved into the metal. The artifact was carefully wrapped in silk cloth before being packed away.
6114. A wooden case containing two dozen bars of sealing wax.
6115. A wooden case which must have belonged to a forger, smuggler, or black marketeer. It contains templates for certificates, and tools for modifying or copying official seals facilitating the creation of counterfeit documents.
6116. A wooden chalice etched with a horrifying visage and topped with a bone covering.
6117. A wooden coin that feels and sounds like metal
6118. A wooden collar that, when worn, makes the bearer absolutely certain they can communicate with trees. The collar does not actually grant this power.
6119. A wooden crate filled with small burlap sacks of different exotic coffee beans
6120. A wooden cup that, when placed against an ear, relays the sounds of a drunken tavern party.
6121. A wooden cup, divided in half lengthwise with a sheet of aluminum.
6122. A wooden flute from a birch tree from the feywild that sprouts small leafy branches.
6123. A wooden flute made of red wood with etchings of leaves around part of its base
6124. A wooden hand cranked coffin shaped box that, when opened, reveals a set of domino tiles made of grey bones.
6125. A wooden handled, iron claw hammer
6126. A wooden jeweler box with copper detailing.
6127. A wooden mask carved to look like the face of a man, but which has been fitted for something wholly other.
6128. A wooden mask with empty eyes that drink in the light and whose jagged grin suggests it was violently carved. It is almost as if the splintered smile was forced upon it when it didn’t laugh of its own accord.
6129. A wooden mask, designed to fit a human face. It is crafted from a pale wood, and has two red streaks covering the left eye hole. The mask has no nose or mouth openings and has no strap. After a creature holds it in place for one minute, it will sit comfortably on their face, regardless of any movement until the bearer wishes for it to fall off.
6130. A wooden model of a ship constructed around a wine bottle so that the bottle is still visible in there. It would probably be more impressive if it was the other way around.
6131. A wooden mouse carving, that when coming into contact with cheese makes a ear-piercing squeak.
6132. A wooden peg-leg in the shape of a dragon’s leg complete with splayed-toed clawed foot tipped with hooked claws of iron.
6133. A wooden pipe carved to resemble a dragon’s head, coloured with bright gold and red paint. It allow the user to reliably smoke a smooth pull, and doubles the amount of smoke the user exhales.
6134. A wooden plate with a drawing of the Sunrise Home, the dwelling place of the Lord of Dawn.
6135. A wooden prosthetic hand with a skull and crossbones etched into the back of the hand. Knowledgeable PC’s will notice that the initials at the base of the limb match those of an infamous one-handed pirate.
6136. A wooden prosthetic leg with a hidden compartment inside of it
6137. A wooden prosthetic leg, carved to appear as a standing leopard with green agate eyes.
6138. A wooden relic carved from bronzewood, in the shape of a serpent. It can always adjust to fit snugly around the bearer’s forearm. It writhes occasionally, when seen out of the corner of your eye.
6139. A wooden scroll case containing various documents outlining the holdings of the Ravencrest family.
6140. A wooden scroll case full of maps of various dungeons. Several maps are unmarked, but none of them seem to match the local area.
6141. A wooden scroll tube containing a bearer-bond that allows the possessor to take ownership of a dozen slaves from Zoealage the flesh-monger.
6142. A wooden scroll tube containing a dozen sheet of parchment, covered with one inch grids.
6143. A wooden scroll tube containing a large piece of rolled up parchment with a detailed sketch of a dark and foreboding castle
6144. A wooden scroll tube containing a parchment scroll, sealed with wax that is an invitation to the Yew-Lord’s banquet. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize the title of the Yew-Lord, as a powerful Arch-Fey.
6145. A wooden scroll tube containing an invitation to visit an amazonian tribe. There is a map burned with arcane magic into the letter. It’s painful (But possible) to read, causing sharp stabbing head pains whenever a creature is looking directly at the map.
6146. A wooden scroll tube containing the deed to a bear sanctuary in a nearby land.
6147. A wooden scrollcase containing a deed to a tract of 40 acres of farmland and low hills which border the gnoll lands and are routinely raided.
6148. A wooden spinning top that looks crudely carved. Yet when it’s spun, it catches the carving in such a manner as to make the sound of children laughing or crying in the distance, depending on which direction the top is spun
6149. A wooden spool around which is wrapped 20 feet of copper wire.
6150. A wooden spool with three feet of coiled copper wire.
6151. A wooden staff that ends in a large loop, with a large and dense net attached to the loop. It looks as though it is primarily designed to catch butterflies, bees, or other such diminutive creatures.
6152. A wooden staff which has a hard to find hollow compartment. Inside is found a scroll which entitles the owner to an inheritance to be collected in a well-known city.
6153. A wooden stein carved with the likeness of an orcish barmaid
6154. A wooden sword sized for a child.
6155. A wooden talisman carved into a screaming human face that when stared at it for more than a few seconds the observer can almost hear the sound of screaming from far off.
6156. A wooden teapot with brass and wood inlays with a handle made from a vitrified monkey hand.
6157. A wooden travel case containing a dozen writing quills made from vulture feathers.
6158. A wooden travel case containing an alchemist’s alembic; An alchemical still consisting of two glass vessels connected by a tube, used for distilling chemicals
6159. A wooden travel case holding three spent wands coddled in velvet lining. They were crafted by the finest artisans and enchanted by the mightiest wizards of a bygone age, but now reduced to mere wood, bone, and glass respectively.
6160. A wooden travel trunk. Inside the case is a layer of padding and a glass display case. Inside the sealed case is a collection of dead butterflies pinned to a painted cork board. Under each specimen is a piece of parchment detailing the common and scientific name of the insect. There are dozens of butterflies of varying sizes and colors.
6161. A wooden tribal mask with sharp teeth, glowing yellow eyes and a beard of leaves.
6162. A wooden tube with a creature carved on it. Every few days, the creature moves into a different position.
6163. A wooden walking stick which has five dragon heads carved into the thick knot at the top of the staff.
6164. A wooden whistle carved in the shape of a wolf, when blown it gives out a low howling sound.
6165. A wooden whistle that imitates the roar of a T-Rex.
6166. A wooden witch doctor’s mask, trimmed with bright feathers and two horns made of the teeth of a large cat. The mask has three painted eyes and a beak instead of a mouth.
6167. A wooden, roughly carved hunting horn that still has patches of bark remaining on its surface. The deep, haunting sound it makes is dark, foreboding, and above all else, wild, with its notes echoing far longer than they should.
6168. A wooden, toy rocking horse, carved to look like a horse whose skin has been removed, muscles, tendons, and blood vessels are all intricately shown. In some areas, even those layers have been removed in favor of exposing parts of the horses skeleton.
6169. A woodsman hat made of animal skin. While worn it grants the user random useless knowledge focusing on plants, animals, weather, geography and nature. The information is only rarely accurate but the user cannot determine what’s true or not.
6170. A woody knob from the end of a wizard’s staff. Creature’s holding the knob feel the urge to sing bawdy tavern songs.
6171. A woolen flat cap hat that stays dry despite any rain or snow.
6172. A woollen scarf that is knitted with the words of an ancient elven prayer.
6173. A workaday leather tunic that draws closed at the neck with antler toggles. The long tunic is slit at the sides to mid-thigh for free movement.
6174. A worm made of rust in a small wooden box. It’s alive and feeds on small amount of metal.
6175. A worn and bloody apron. One of it’s pockets holds a small knife, a whetstone and a small vial of salt.
6176. A worn blacksmith’s hammer, that twitches when near other sources of worked metals.
6177. A worn but well-polished, silver inlaid gavel.
6178. A worn letter closed with blood red wax wax and sealed with an stress inducing symbol in the shape of a down turned crescent marked with a few inward-facing spikes. Breaking the seal and reading the contents reveals a letter from an unknown author to their unnamed relative begging for their aid during a time of dire need. The pleading note reads as follows: “Ruin has come to our family. You remember our venerable house, opulent and imperial, gazing proudly from its stoic perch above the moor. I lived all my years in that ancient rumor shadowed manor, fattened by decadence and luxury, and yet I began to tire of… conventional extravagance. Singular unsettling tales suggested the mansion itself was a gateway to some fabulous and unnameable power. With relic and ritual, I bent every effort towards the excavation and recovery of those long buried secrets, exhausting what remained of our family fortune on… swarthy workmen and… sturdy shovels. At last, in the salt soaked crags beneath the lowest foundations, we unearthed that damnable portal of antediluvian evil. Our every step unsettled the ancient earth, but we were in a realm of death and madness. In the end, I alone fled, laughing and wailing through those blackened arcades of antiquity. Until consciousness failed me. You remember our venerable house, opulent and imperial. It is a festering abomination! I beg you, return home, claim your birthright and deliver our family from the ravenous clutching shadows of the Darkest Dungeon.”
6179. A worn looking banjo with the peg head made out of a carved piece of driftwood.
6180. A worn looking red building brick. If held up to an ear, the sounds of a busy city can be heard.
6181. A worn mercenary banner consisting of one rusty old spearhead atop a long wooden shaft. Five feet down from the head there rests a cross-piece four feet long tied to the shaft. From that hangs flag itself; A field of scarlet with nine hanged men in black and six yellow daggers in the upper left and lower right quadrants, respectively, while the upper right quadrant features a shattered skull and the lower left boasts a bird of prey astride a severed head. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize it as the Standard of the Black Company a free mercenary company who can trace their history back hundreds of years through their well-documented archives.
6182. A worn playing card depicting an unsightly old woman with knobbled fingers peering over her shoulder towards the viewer, smiling with unholy glee, her jagged and misplaced teeth creating a haunting smile. When the bearer blinks, the figure is replaced with the viewer, looking fearful and bewildered.
6183. A worn smooth worry stone. When rubbed in the bearer’s hand, it makes a soft, reassuring, purring sound that only the bearer can hear.
6184. A worn-looking box of dark wood, fitted with simple hinges of brass, is roughly the size of a man’s head, and rattles when moved. The interior contains a collection of tiny humanoid bones. The underside of the lid bears writing that appears in the primary language of anyone reading it, and states that the most recent reader is attuned with the box. No further explanation is to be found within.
6185. A worn, brass key nearly a handspan wide and decorated with thorny vines.
6186. A writing quill ending in a grasping hand rather than a sharpened tip.
6187. A writing slate (1’ x 2’) and a box containing ten pieces of white chalk.
6188. A written note saying, in what looks like a child’s handwriting, “You’d better stop doing what you’re doing. It’ll get you killed…or worse.”
6189. A wyvern’s stinger
6190. A yellow ceramic plate in the shape of the sun
6191. A yellowing, delicate, paper fan with a paisley pattern laid on it in gold leaf
6192. An abrasive steel cord with metal handles on both ends of it. It can be used as a portable lightweight saw or as a garrote depending on the owner’s needs.
6193. An acceptance letter to a school of magic
6194. An accordion made brown suede of with bronze keys.
6195. An acorn that makes ringing noises and vibrates when touched.
6196. An acorn with little green sprouts that retreat back into the nut when exposed to sunlight.
6197. An agate scarab the size of a human palm with writing in Mulhorandi that reads “Even in death, I serve”.
6198. An age-worn ivory figurine which bears an uncanny resemblance to a young woman the PCs have met recently.
6199. An aged obsidian fruit knife with bronze handle.
6200. An aged papyrus scroll bearing the image of an open sarcophagus, its lid propped up against the side. In its contents are gemstones, jewelry, and even precious coin lain in among the rotting silks of long dead corpse. Wafts of green and brown can be seen emanating from the molded cloths, and around the open container the carcasses of sweltering animals and humans remain motionless as the plague eats away at the flesh.
6201. An aged stone tablet containing fragments of text of the Sargonne Prophecies. The fragments speaks of “…an armada of horrors swollen and grey and hungry so hungry so thirsty, they know, they KNOW, THEY KNOW…”
6202. An aged, battle horn, of the sort used by ancient warrior tribes. The finely detailed engraving on such a horn depicts a pattern of bones and skulls.
6203. An alabaster mask
6204. An alabaster vase that has bas-relief figures of goddesses in skimpy clothing in provocative poses. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the goddesses are in fact the handmaidens of the Martyr Prophet and even to depict them clothed is a right arrogated to the Prophet’s priesthood. The vase itself would be counted a blasphemy by the Prophet’s followers.
6205. An amalgam of dozen small animal skulls, each from a different creature, all compressed and partly melted into a heavy, fist-sized ball of horror.
6206. An amber belt buckle with knot pattern.
6207. An amber brooch in the shape of a turtle. When worn by women capable of magic, the amber seems to glow with a barely perceptible light.
6208. An amber prism with a prehistoric beetle trapped inside
6209. An amphora made of red clay, that always feels hot to the touch.
6210. An ancient and battered Ouija board.
6211. An ancient and ornate bronze oil lamp. It’s badly tarnished and in need of a thorough cleaning. From time to time, the lamp seems to creak of its own accord.
6212. An ancient animal skin drum yellowed and worn with age. It naturally hums softly to the rhythm of nature’s heartbeat.
6213. An ancient arrow of elven design
6214. An ancient bronze coin from an ancient city that was destroyed by natural disaster
6215. An ancient bronze coin minted by a long fallen empire. The face of the tyrant stamped on it looks exactly like one of the PC’s.
6216. An ancient copper bell with a remarkably musical chime
6217. An ancient cup, now cracked and chipped, that was carefully carved out of a single large block of translucent red amber.
6218. An ancient drinking horn crafted from some extinct beast. Those who drink from it have dreams of being a huge predator roaming unchallenged through a prehistoric landscape.
