6219. An ancient hieroglyphic tablet made out of some type of reflective metal that can float in water and makes a strange echoing hollow sound when struck.
6220. An ancient map of a legendary library believed to have sunk into the desert.
6221. An ancient relic in the shape of a stone hand, the origins and purpose of which has been forgotten through time.
6222. An ancient scrimshaw with a well carved boat labelled, “The Mourning Hag.”
6223. An ancient set of pipes made from the hollowed out finger bones of a dead bard, whose soul is still bound to them. When music is played from the instrument, listeners can faintly make out a gentle vocal accompaniment that perfectly fits whatever is being played.
6224. An ancient stone tablet on which is chiseled a prayer dedicated to an elder God of madness and destruction, who currently sleeps beneath the waves of the deepest ocean. The words can be read and understood by any sentient creature regardless of their linguistic skills. The prayer reads more like a children’s lullaby and attempt to soothe the elder being’s slumber and keep the deity dormant in his stone house, cradled by dark waters. Knowledgeable PCs will know that the elder god has no true temple or shrine to its name and its only worshipers are deranged cultists. Wise PC’s will realize that a substantial translation enchantment has been placed on this tablet and that perhaps reciting the prayer now and again would be prudent, lest the cosmic horror stir from its hibernation and rise again.
6225. An ancient world map that appears to show the entire land was green and fertile.
6226. An ancient, acid-pitted doubloon with a kraken on one side and a ship on the other
6227. An ancient, palm sized fertility sculpture carved of stone.
6228. An angelic looking feathered mask affixed to the end of a wooden stick. If held by a creature in front of their own face, it grants them visions of their innermost fears
6229. An angular carved crystal vial holds a thin light blue liquid. The crystal vial feels chilled to the touch and when let sit for long periods of time it forms crystals throughout the liquid that quickly dissipate when disturbed again.
6230. An ankh made of carved human bone.
6231. An ankle bracelet that sometimes eases muscle aches
6232. An anklet crafted from jade with hieroglyphic warnings of dire things to come.
6233. An anklet made from fresh liquid blood, held together by odd magic.
6234. An anklet of braided gold and silver worked with small carnelians.
6235. An antiquated torture device designed for mutilating hands and fingers.
6236. An antique crystal perfume dispenser with a hose and squeeze pump. The crystal reservoir is overlaid by a pewter octopus with human eyes. A green liquid can be seen inside.
6237. An antique pipe that has a carved parrot wearing a tricorn hat perched on its bowl. The smoke that billows from the chamber is colored a wild mix of reds, blues, and greens.
6238. An antique tortoiseshell hairbrush.
6239. An antique wooden box engraved with a forest scene.
6240. An antler from an unknown creature that continues to slowly grow
6241. An antler from an unknown creature that emits clicking noises when near children
6242. An apparent treasure map of the local area, written in an uncommon language with cryptic script and strange markings. It clearly holds the key to discovering something of major value to anyone who can decipher what it is saying.
6243. An apparently empty glass jar with a white wood lid. Any attempt to twist the lid loose is immediately met with a loud hissing noise, as if the jar is under extreme pressure.
6244. An apple that is the most mouth-watering, beautiful apple you have ever seen…but when you approach within three feet of it you begin to see it rot and spoil right in front of your eyes and even touching it and smelling it confirms that it is indeed rotted. As you back away you see the apple’s rot and decay reverse and it becomes the same beautiful apple you saw seconds earlier.
6245. An arcane wand that is rough to hold and twists like a wild vine.
6246. An archery target three feet in diameter, made of straw filled canvas with a wooden back. The canvas it painted with alternating red and white circles with a black bull’s eye and its center.
6247. An armored satchel that provides total cover and protection to any cat sized or smaller creature contained within it. It includes air holes (Which can be plugged with cork stoppers in areas of toxic atmosphere.) and two built in receptacles for food and water. This type of container is most often used by mages to keep their familiars safe during adventures.
6248. An armoured gorget consisting of a metal throat-shield and a series of overlapping metal plates that encircle the neck. The entire device is connected to a belt made of leather. When worn, the battle gorget protects the wearer from physical strangulation and bites to the neck such as the case if they were hung by a noose or attacked by a vampire.
6249. An arrest warrant for the constable’s daughter of the local town.
6250. An artfully sculpted memorial urn in the shape of a cat’s head. In an elegant script on a flat area near the bottom is etched; “In loving memory of one taken too soon.
6251. An artfully sculpted memorial urn in the shape of an angel in flight. In an elegant script on a flat area near the bottom is etched; “If loved could have saved you, you would have lived forever”.
6252. An artificial fibula made of rusted, wrought iron delicately carved with scrollwork and decorative rivets; all nearly obscured in red oxidization.
6253. An artificial hand carved of bogwood with silver inlays indicating the location of every bone in the wrist, palm, and fingers; all labeled in a looping, unknown script.
6254. An artificial moth crafted of jade slivers and twists of silver wire and trapped in a squat, glass bottle. The strange construct will fly briefly and feebly if warmed by a living palm.
6255. An artificial nose made of copper that has a leather thong attached to it.
6256. An ash divining rod that twitches at random
6257. An ash wood walking stick
6258. An astrologer’s chart, inscribed with the finest instruments and the greatest precision upon a supple vellum sheet. Knowledgeable PC’s will discover that these charts were constructed around an entirely different night sky and possibly a calendrical system and length of year unlike anything known upon this world.
6259. An astrological chart with alien characters drawn in silver ink.
6260. An attractive statuette depicting an androgynous figure standing with one arm covering the chest and the other covering the groin. Made from smooth jade, it is an exquisite piece of art, but an insightful PC can sense that there is more to this idol, something far more wicked than its appearance would suggest
6261. An auroch skin sleeping bag that protects from the worst of the cold.
6262. An automaton crab. If wound up with the key in its brass carapace, it will menace any nearby animals with its snappy little mechanical claws.
6263. An axe head of exceptional craftsmanship whose faces are inlaid with a faience scene showing a crowd of human villagers throwing a halfling down a well.
6264. An azure steel spring that takes a remarkable amount of effort to compress.
6265. An bouquet of funerary flowers that always looks and smells fresh
6266. An ebon walking stick with a monogrammed silver handle.
6267. An ebony canister sealed with wax. The canister is filled with ash, in the middle of which are a pair of pulsating purple pods connected together by slick, black tendrils.
6268. An ebony carved walking cane with tiger head; the head has a hidden jaw with space within to hold a small object such as a potion vial or scroll. The interior hinge is fashioned from a preserved human finger.
6269. An ebony statuette of a sphinx with three faces.
6270. An ebony statuette of a standing bear of exquisite workmanship. A small fairy ring of mushrooms cast in silver rests in the base
6271. An ebony walking stick.
6272. An eccentric plum coloured hat decorated with a silver buckle and a long egret feather that droops over the bearer’s eyes.
6273. An echo pearl from the depths of the Vibration Lake
6274. An eel-hide leather pouch filled with an unspeakably foul-tasting coarse salt.
6275. An eerie mask carved from bone to resemble the gaunt face of a terrifying vampire whose expression is that of inhuman malice.
6276. An eerily lifelike wooden bear figurine that is never found where it is left
6277. An egg-shaped metallic bauble that occasionally spins and speaks in a language no one understands
6278. An egg-shaped stone that, when cracked open, squeals and then puts itself back together
6279. An egg-sized smooth white stone poorly carved on one side to resemble a grinning skull. The tiny eye sockets appear far deeper than should be possible on an item this size.
6280. An egg, roughly the size of a goose egg but navy blue with mottled flecks of gold leaf, mounted on a round wooden base with a tiny placard that reads Imaskari Sun Hawk”. When touched, the golden flecks on the egg gently glow that grows brighter and softer in time with the heartbeat of the one touching it and there is the sensation of rustling movement from within.
6281. An eight inch wide roll of silk, which when unfurled is revealed to be an elaborately decorated sock kite in the shape of a koi.
6282. An eight inch, braided leather cord, adorned by a single, polished silver bead.
6283. An eight-inch-tall, wrist thick cinnamon-scented candle that’s never been lit.
6284. An elaborate, high-necked bustier laced and lined with black silk and adorned with sapphires and beljurils.
6285. An elaborate, whalebone scrimshaw carving of intertwined orbs, each showing the legend of creation story seven different civilized religions, roughly one-foot in diameter.
6286. An elaborately braided bicorne made of rich blue felt, embroidered with golden thread. It has a showy badge called a cockade, proclaiming nationality, faith, family crest, and the like.
6287. An elaborately carved white jade smithing hammer, whose purpose is no doubt ceremonial rather than practical.
6288. An elegant wand constructed a red oak shaft, a burnished brass handle and a flame tongued tip of red jade.
6289. An elegant yet simple flute carved from a single piece of ebony wood.
6290. An elegantly carved ashwood comb, engraved with the name “Ilthorien” and decorated with twin unicorns.
6291. An elegantly carved oaken comb, engraved with the name “Aethelren” and decorated with twin dragons.
6292. An elephant shaped statuette, carved from the tusk of the animal that it resembles.
6293. An elongated, angular mask designed for masquerade balls. It features a large pair of velveted antlers and a crown of lustrous ivy.
6294. An elven hunting cap that’s especially elongated, visually mirroring an elf’s elongated, pointed ears. It’s black, and the brim is pinned on one side with a tourmaline brooch, holding an iridescent peryton feather.
6295. An elven poncho covered in long strands that resemble weeping willow, wisteria and ivy vines.
6296. An embroidered claret-coloured jacket decorated with brass buttons on the front and on the sleeves.
6297. An embroidered silk tablecloth edged with lace.
6298. An emerald green silk turban with a black border. One end is intended to hang loosely over the shoulder and has five long tassels alternately coloured green and white.
6299. An empty black box from which issue faint calls of “Hello?”
6300. An empty crystal phial that glows with a strange blue light.
6301. An empty djinn lamp. There is a note attached on which are written the words: “It’s out. It is coming for you.”
6302. An empty glass vial that smells of perfume when opened
6303. An empty green wine bottle made of seemingly indestructible glass.
6304. An empty metallic scroll tube that projects a set of illusionary schematics for building a massive clockwork dragon when opened.
6305. An empty scabbard with an intricate design etched into the leather. With enough study, it may be interpreted as a map that purports to lead to the location of the matching sword.
6306. An empty section of honeycomb. It causes an almost electric tingle if touched to bare skin.
6307. An empty silver snuffbox bearing an inscription on the surface that says “dreams”
6308. An empty teak jewelry box that has been sealed with wax
6309. An empty wine bottle bearing a pretty label that says, “The Wizard of Wines Winery, Red Dragon Crush, 331422-W”
6310. An empty, crumpled leather belt pouch with a large hole opened along the bottom seam and a snarling beast embossed on the side.
6311. An empty, six inch long crystal coffin that currently has no occupant.
6312. An engraved geode that has been split apart into two equal halves. A labyrinth is carved into the rock’s rough exterior and glows with a faint light.
6313. An enormous scale, perhaps from a dragon
6314. An enormous star sapphire, well over a hundred carats, but of very poor quality. The colour varies from butty gray to muddy brown, and there are concentric rings crossing the material and several black inclusions and imperfections.
6315. An envelope stuffed with cheap woodcut prints of men in various states of undress.
6316. An envelope, wax sealed with the mark of Lady Farris, the infamous Tax Collector of Weatherbrund.
6317. An envelope, yellow and weathered, but still sealed with wax pressed by a local legate’s signet.
6318. An expertly taxidermied cat with built in candle holders capable of bearing three large candles. The cat is posed curled up, as if it was sleeping.
6319. An expertly taxidermied dog with built in candle holders capable of bearing three large candles. The dog is posed sitting up, as if begging for scraps.
6320. An expired potion of darkvision, that tastes heavily of carrots. If consumed, the drinker’s eyes become sensitive to light for one hour.
6321. An expired potion of magical growth, that tastes heavily of iron. If consumed, the drinker’s lips double in size for one minute.
6322. An exquisite flute made from a human thigh bone.
6323. An exquisite looking butter knife with the name “Balduran” engraved on the handle. A knowledgeable PC will remember the legends of Balduran’s Butter Knife as the hero would allow no other utensil to touch his condiments. It is believed that his mother gave the ornate butter knife to him as an 18th birthday gift. The knife is well made but otherwise mundane and it could be that Balduran was simply a picky eater.
6324. An exquisite scrimshaw design of dueling dragons made from a harpy claw.
6325. An exquisitely crafted red ceramic cup, incised with black figures depicting lions, wolves, and fruiting plants.
6326. An exquisitely preserved insect fossil the length of a grown man’s arm.
6327. An extraordinarily fine miniature saddle, as though for a small dog, worked in supple leather and decorated with gold and silver thread.
6328. An extremely large leaf that causes food that it touches to taste very bitter.
6329. An extremely vivid and detailed portrait of the PCs going about their day, that seems to have been painted within the last week.
6330. An extremely wide brimmed wizard’s hat
6331. An eyepatch resembling a large flower that covers the entire eye of the creature wearing it. When applied to a creature’s face, the eyepatch grows rootlike tendrils that wrap around the bearer’s head to secure it.
6332. An eyepatch that when worn, shows a faint golden glow around certain individuals at random.
6333. An hand sized coral figurine of a merman wielding a two-bladed sword
6334. An hourglass that always takes a different amount of time to empty, but never an hour.
6335. An hourglass that has something golden hidden in the sands, but before the object is revealed, the device always turns itself over to hide the treasure beneath more pouring sand. Smashing the hourglass reveals only sand within.
6336. An hourglass, slightly melted, set in a drooping, ancient housing of twisted glass. Within the bloated glass bulbs, there are two, separate liquids: A clear, slightly yellow oil that fills the space like air, and a heavy, cherry-red liquid separate from it. This cherry liquid acts as sand, dripping in slow globules from the top bulb to the bottom. Despite its age, it keeps a perfect hour.
6337. An illustration of a silver tabby cat with these words on what seems to be a flyer: “Lost kitty. Responds to the name ‘Mankiller.’ Please return to Alda’s Alchemy Shoppe if found. Reward provided—higher if alive. Do not feed or raise from the dead. He bites; wounds will be treated. You kill him, you will be cursed and haunted by something that is utterly unholy and has a somewhat questionable sense of humour.”
6338. An incense holder carved in the likeness of a silt horror
6339. An incredibly detailed drawing of an alien creature.
6340. An incredibly life-like sandstone statue of a cockatiel.
6341. An incredibly realistic human finger carved entirely from ivory.
6342. An incredibly soft pillow that sometimes cuts your hair while you sleep
6343. An incredibly uncomfortable vest made of goat and horse hair, sackcloth, loose metal wires and twigs commonly known as a hairshirt. Knowledgeable PC’s know that garments like these are typically worn by those with religious penitence in mind. The constant pain and discomfort is supposed to purify the soul and wash away (Or atone for) sin.
6344. An indecipherable treasure map
6345. An indestructible human skull that mumbles racist slurs whenever it sees an elf.
6346. An infant’s ceramic rattle containing human teeth
6347. An inflamed gland that’s little more than an engorged, muscular lump, radiating heat. The repellent hunk of flesh pulses with a warm, wet heat like an exhaled breath. The gland’s presence consumes the senses. One’s head begins to ache with a feverish intensity the longer it is held.
6348. An ink black statuette of a beautiful woman, clothed in gossamer-like veils, holds a large bronze bowl.
6349. An ink dip pen made entirely of tiny bones, complete with matching ink pot.
6350. An innocuous-looking, fist-sized piece of faceted glass. When placed upon a flat surface the object floats about three feet into the air, glowing and chiming softly.
6351. An intricate eyeglass shaped in the likeness of a yellow cat’s eye.
6352. An intricate feather made entirely out of a single piece of clear glass.
6353. An intricate metal contraption which clamps to a table top and consists of a small vise, several articulated arms with lenses, clamps, and unidentified doodads. The entire thing is covered in small coils and loops as though to hold a vast array of tiny tools.
6354. An intricate wooden box with delicate gold filigree and a wind up key on the back. If it is opened after having been wound-up a beautiful melody plays out.
6355. An intricately articulated wooden figure of a multi-headed dog covered in a mouldy gray fur.
6356. An intricately carved scrimshaw serpent that wraps its way around a large snake fang.
6357. An intricately carved wooden juju stick
6358. An intricately carved wooden shaman mask
6359. An intricately made harp, inlaid with several glassy gray gemstones of various types.
6360. An intricately miniature version of a strange town contained in a snowglobe, every now and again lights flicker in the tiny houses.
6361. An invisible whoopee cushion that becomes visible for one minute after a creature sits on it.
6362. An invitation to a charity ball rewarded for substantial devotion and contribution to community and individual well being.
6363. An invitation to a formal ball to be held in two years time
6364. An invitation to a magician’s circle on a date that doesn’t quite make sense.
6365. An invitation to a party that’s taking place a month from now. The party takes place in a nearby city and the invitation will admit the bearer and a plus one.
6366. An invitation to a party where a murder happened
6367. An iron beard comb of dwarvish design.
6368. An iron blacksmith’s mold for crafting an arrowhead
6369. An iron bracelet shaped like a coiled serpent with rhodochrosite eyes.
6370. An iron coin with an evil sigil on one side and the face of a demon on the other, flipping it causes the holder to hear a deep malevolent laugh.
6371. An iron disc untouched by rust. It is three inches in diameter and is engraved with a spiral on both sides.
6372. An iron flask inlaid with runes of silver and stoppered by a brass plug bearing a seal engraved with sigils and glyphs of imprisonment.
6373. An iron gorget with a large peridot in the center of it.
6374. An iron holy symbol devoted to an unknown god
6375. An iron hook, barbed, like those meant for fishing, but quite too large. Two links of rusted, cast iron chain trail from it, followed by a flat, similarly cast tag of iron. It shows, under ample rust, the simple etching of a bony man hung by a hook sunk through his collarbone. Three runes, like circles cut with spurs, are stamped below. The tag has another hole opposite the attachment of its links, suggesting more where attached thereafter.
6376. An iron horseshoe once worn by a unicorn.
6377. An iron horseshoe which makes a slight humming sound at all times.
6378. An iron key. According to the engraving on the handle, the key is used to unlock the front gate of a local church’s graveyard.
6379. An iron lantern forged to resemble a human skull in size an shape. The top of the skull is attached to a foot of chain ending in a hand sized iron hook.
6380. An iron mask resembling a skull with its mouth sowed shut.
6381. An iron nail from the crucifixion of a prophet.
6382. An iron orb that always rolls against gravity, but only while in contact with a solid surface. If not in contact with a solid surface, it has no unusual properties.
6383. An iron plated dwarf skull.
6384. An iron pocket watch with the chain extruding from an eagle’s mouth mounted into the top of the watch. The clasp at the end of the chain is a talon.
6385. An iron pot who’s handle never gets hot.
6386. An iron rose of spectacular craftsmanship.
6387. An iron skillet who’s handle never gets hot.
6388. An iron slave collar with cruel spikes affixed to the inside.
6389. An iron splitting wedge meant to split wood with a maul
6390. An iron torc with six small silver engravings: A hare, a horse, a spear, a wolf’s head, a simple circle, and an oak tree.
6391. An iron tube containing a long scroll inscribed with the ideology of a forbidden cult.
6392. An iron weather vane in the shape of a rooster
6393. An iron-bound bullhorn
6394. An ironwood skeleton key inlaid with spiraling lines of silvery mithril, and etched with flowing Sylvan script that reads “May this world know some measure of my skill as I depart to the next.”
6395. An irreparably broken steel dagger with some salvageable silver insets
6396. An item that appears at first glance to be no more than a simple burlap doll with no hair or clothes in the shape of humanoid. The doll’s wicked grin and black sapphire eyes tell keen observers a different tale.
6397. An ivory and copper smoking pipe with stale tobacco inside.
6398. An ivory bracelet in the shape of an acrobat bent over backwards so her wrists are tied to his ankles.
6399. An ivory comb, the kind meant to lay flat and stay a plate of hair. Blackened, either with age or with purposeful tarnish, and carved on its handle with images of plagued skeletons, obviously undead, spilling over each other in a chilling accurate depiction of frozen, unnaturally insectile movement.
6400. An ivory disk engraved with an image of a grinning skull and inlaid with black enamel to form a shield-shaped background.
6401. An ivory flute mouthpiece, noticeably lacking in the rest of the instrument. When held to the mouth, the rest of the flute coalesces. Its ghostly blue form is semisolid and always produces sweet but strangely sad notes.
6402. An ivory hair pin with a set of fluttering wings attached to it
6403. An ivory mask that has been fashioned into the likeness of a human skull.
6404. An ivory pipe carved in the shape of a crocodile.
6405. An ivory plague mask, the beak stuffed with fragrant potpourri.
6406. An ivory playing piece shaped like the bust of a muscled, eyeless man with interlocked fingers and palms rested atop the pummel of some weapon haft. He is eyeless, for the metal dome of a lead skullcap covers his ears, nose and eyes. Only thin lips show below.
6407. An ivory scroll case with silk bands and silver plated caps.
6408. An ivory spoon with teardrop handle.
6409. An ivory stamp used for wax sealing letters bearing the heraldic marks of an ancient and forgotten ruler.
6410. An ivory statuette of a mermaid that is for the most part crudely carved, with the exception of certain 'features’ which have been carved with lavish detail.
6411. An oak wood pipe with writing carved on its side in infernal. It reads “Let he who smokes from the pipe be damned”.
6412. An oaken backscratcher set with four cabochon-cut rectangular jade pieces.
6413. An object made from a cross piece of wood and many colors of brightly woven yarn in a geometric pattern vaguely resembling an eye. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as a “God’s Eye” which is a symbol of the power of seeing and understanding that which is unknown and unknowable. The four points represent the elemental processes: earth, fire, air, and water. The weaving of a God’s Eye is an ancient contemplative and spiritual practice for many peoples.
6414. An object that can’t be accurately described. When someone tries to describe it, they’re at a loss for words about even its most basic shape, color, weight or purpose.
6415. An obsidian comb that makes hair slightly curlier when used.
6416. An obsidian figurine in the shape of a crouching cat waiting to pounce on its next victim. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize the symbol etched into the bottom of the carving as the sigil of Felis-Onca, a demon spirit of the feline lycanthrope god
6417. An obsidian figurine of a stalking panther that holds absolutely no magic powers or enchantments.
6418. An obsidian icon of a forgotten deity.
6419. An obsidian jewelry box with a black rose embroidered on it. The box is all sharp edges, oppressively heavy and has a jagged uneven surface.
6420. An obsidian statuette of a leering gargoyle.
6421. An obsidian tablet the width and height of a human hand upon which when viewed under the night sky tiny green and blue dots appear to move.
6422. An ocarina carved from a large acorn, with a wyrm carved in relief.
6423. An ocarina made out of a large beetle exoskeleton. When played, the sound is not dissimilar to a cricket’s chirp.
6424. An ocarina seemingly crafted from snowflake obsidian that produces some decently low notes and is shaped somewhat like an aquatic animal of some sort.
6425. An octagon-shaped amulet that is colored black during the day and sky blue at night
6426. An octagonal dinner plate that fills with unknown writing whenever a creature speaks in its vicinity
6427. An odd contraption comprised of a small crystal orb set within a thick metal semi-sphere, covering most of the orb, and is about six inches across.
6428. An odd little brush which carefully cleans any surface it is applied to, leaving it smelling vaguely of camphor.
6429. An odd receipt of a business transaction where a dragonborn adventurer wearing a full suit of ebony armor sold the shopkeeper salvaged bones collected from a half dozen slain dragons and bought 638 wheels of cheese in return.
6430. An odd stone that seems to permeate magical energy.
6431. An oddly detailed drawing of a pack of wolves chasing a small cloaked child. The numbers six, one, and two are arranged in a equilateral triangle pattern with the six being on the point above the wolves. Strange symbols are on the corners of the page.
6432. An oddly formed stone that is strongly magnetic to non-ferrous metals
6433. An oddly shaped curved wand with elven writing carved within. When held at nighttime it helps its owner sleep peacefully to the sounds of nature.
6434. An oddly shaped metal cap, covered with strange, angular patterns of what seems to be the most minute engraving. It was much too thin to be of use as a helmet and is twice as heavy as it appears.
6435. An oddly shaped, small stuffed toy depicting a goblin, sometimes it seems to wink at the owner.
6436. An ode to Genial Jack, the Godwhale, who swims the Sixty Seas with the city of Jackburg on His back and in His belly. Scribbled on the back is a mysterious phrase: “The tongues of the dead wag at midnight.”
6437. An off-white canvas bag with a green and bronze dragon embroidered on it. It always smells of a campfire that has just been put out.
6438. An oil lamp made from a turtle’s shell embellished with gold leaf and a copper handle.
6439. An oil lamp no larger than two cupped hands that’s both delicate and fearful. Unlike more common lamps of brass or even common earthenware, the lamp is forged of hair-thin and glittering black iron, cool to the touch. It bears a single looped handle, and is covered in finely rendered etchings of arabesques and stylized wings.
6440. An oil lantern whose chimney is made of a deep red glass.
6441. An old abacus with strange characters carved onto each of the beads.
6442. An old and beaten up steel drinking flask. After carrying the object for more than 1d4 hours, the bearer becomes convinced that the flask has to be kept a secret.
6443. An old brass lamp in which an genie once resided. His name is inscribed on the rim in Ignan, the language of fire.
6444. An old but seemingly undamaged piece of abstract art containing a pattern designed to befuddle the viewer’s brain.
6445. An old chess piece made from glass
6446. An old copper door knocker fashioned to resemble the face of an eldritch creature. It is always cold to the touch.
6447. An old divination card bearing your likeness
6448. An old glass bottle with a glass stopper sealed with wax. It contains a butty white liquid with ribbons of black and grey suspended in it.
6449. An old harmonica engraved with a compass card and a variety of fish. When played on land it summons a fresh breeze smelling of salt and seaweed, putting everyone within earshot in a melancholic mood and longing for the sea.
6450. An old key
6451. An old lute made of ironwood, with strings and tuning keys of glistening steel.
6452. An old military medal awarded to a soldier who had been injured in battle. The award is in the shape of a heart and is strung on a thick purple ribbon.
6453. An old scratched monocle which shows an underwater landscape whenever looked through.
6454. An old withered hand, no more than skin drawn taut across bones, and tarnished rings hanging loosely from the fingers. The bearer can rattle the rings on the hand which causes the smell of lilies to fills the air around him.
6455. An old yellowed skull that in spite of its lack of eyeballs, seems to be constantly eyeing the bearer.
6456. An old, battered telescope that shows odd luminescent lines linking the stars when it is used.
6457. An old, fraying coat of the type a ship’s captain would wear in bad weather. There is a small singed hole through the outer layers that stops at an inside pocket.
6458. An old, straw-filled ragdoll with a patch above its heart. It is always comfortably warm to the touch.
6459. An old, thick coin, dented but cleanly cast with bas reliefs. Some runes on the obverse; and on the reverse: the cruel-eyed face of a woman with spiral horns. The con has a scent of mildew and copper that rubs off when handled.
6460. An old, worn smith’s hammer. Its head is always hot to the touch.
6461. An onyx carved into a small coin. The obverse sigil is a simple flat line denoting the void. The reverse a phrase in common that reads “No barrier will hold back my anger.”
6462. An onyx comb
6463. An onyx comb shaped like a raven that has common cabochon ruby eyes.
6464. An onyx gemstone that radiates unnatural energy.
6465. An onyx hair pin topped with a golden sphere accented by ivory flowers. It’s covered in dust and the sphere is a bit oxidized, but with some proper cleaning it might be a suitable gift for the daughter of a noble.
6466. An opaque green bottle containing a pale blue liquid that smells of freshly fallen rain.
6467. An opaque jar that cannot be opened or broken
6468. An opaque, black eyepatch that the bearer can see through perfectly.
6469. An ordinary looking black ceramic cup that hides a tiny secret compartment in the thick bottom of the cup which can serve as an excellent place to store a small item or substance. The compartment is designed with a weighted catch that opens when the cup is tilted back, thereby releasing the hidden substance into the contents of the cup. The compartment is hard to spot when the cup is empty and all but impossible to find when filled.
6470. An ordinary looking black scarf containing five chicken eggs firmly secured in secret pockets. Close inspection shows the eggs have been drained of the yolk with two small pinpricks and have been filled with fine powdered ash then resealed with dabs of wax. These eggs can be used as thrown weapons or launched from a sling. Upon impact these eggs explode, throwing up a small butt of ash that quickly dissipates. This can distract creatures, provide partial cover or potentially blind. These homemade smoke grenades are meant for distraction and misdirection rather than as an offensive weapon and don’t deal any damage.
6471. An ordinary looking hens egg that defies all efforts to crack it open or otherwise damage it.
6472. An ordinary looking wooden cased compass that points east at dawn, west and dusk and spins randomly all other times.
6473. An origami dragon made from a (used, blank) spell scroll.
6474. An origami unicorn that can never be torn or crumpled
6475. An ornamental rod of office, bronze topped with a key-like device, roughly four feet long.
6476. An ornate amber hair comb that’s missing a tooth
6477. An ornate black-iron lantern once used by Charon the ferryman of the afterlife, to help guide the dead across the waters of the river of souls. Its light is mixed with the blackened shadows of dead men.
6478. An ornate brass plaque engraved with a history of a crossbow bolt that is supposedly mounted on it. The plaque says that during the siege of castle Châlus by the invading King Richard (Who is described as a warlord who had devastated the nearby lands with fire and sword) a young cook stood on the edge of the castle walls. The boy, not quite yet a man held a crossbow in one hand and a frying pan in the other. The cook fended off stray arrows with his makeshift shield as he lined as target in his sights. King Richard, who was known to take walks unarmored at the edges of the various sieges he lead locked eyes with the boy and applauded his courage. The cook’s aim was true and his bolt found a new quiver in the king’s neck, ending the siege and a reign of terror. It’s a moving story, however the bolt in question is nowhere to be found, although there is a rectangular depression in the plaque where a glass case or display piece should be placed.
6479. An ornate bronze tube, capped on each end with polished horn and wrapped tightly with thick red cord.
6480. An ornate brooch of dwarven design
6481. An ornate bullseye lantern adorned with gold filigree and sun patterns.
6482. An ornate Dwarven flask made of brass.
6483. An ornate glasswork sculpture of a phoenix in all its resplendent glory, its wings spread majestically over the ember glow of an active volcano. The whole piece has been magically enchanted and the illusionary flames around the bird are animated and dance and drift off of the creature’s feathers and the volcano occasionally erupts in showers of harmless sparks. The glass is warm to the touch and is as durable as steel.
6484. An ornate lacquered box containing a set of spoons, thirteen in number. Each is topped with a tiny figure that represents one of the Immortal Heroes of an eastern cult that is thought to be extinct. In that cult, the spoons are considered a valuable prize that proves the courage and skill of its members. The set would be decently valuable to a collector or otherwise interested buyer.
6485. An ornate linen headband with brass decorations.
6486. An ornate mask made of silver and lace, made for the masquerade parties of nobles. The inside of the mask is covered in symbols and images of eyes of all shapes and colors. A creature wearing the mask constantly feels as if they are being watched.
6487. An ornate sacrificial dagger, still stained with the blood of its last victim
6488. An ornate saucer painted with scenes of songbirds in flight. Whenever you aren’t looking directly at it, the birds seem to move.
6489. An ornate scabbard set with agates of multiple colors.
6490. An ornate scabbard that fits no blade you have found so far
6491. An ornate scholar’s brush with a sharp iron handle. It is easily concealed and can be used as a jabbing weapon or thrown short distances dealing half as much damage as a dagger would.
6492. An ornate token from an opulent land. When held, one feels aided by forces paid for dearly. Surely, no cost is too high for triumph?
6493. An ornate urn filled with ashen remains. The markings on the urn signify they are the remains of an infamous religious zealot that was responsible for a holy war that killed thousands.
6494. An ornate wooden box containing two weathered dueling hand crossbows.
6495. An ornate, hand-inlaid, glass globe contained within a protective wicker cradle.
6496. An ornate, tarnished key with two prongs. Neither end seems to be able to open anything.
6497. An ornate, white, wooden coffin sized to fit a newborn
6498. An ornately carved pipe, its bowl fashioned into the head of a satyr; whose expression is one of malicious pleasure. If the pipe is used for smoking tobacco without cleaning it out first, the bearer will be plunged into a vivid, momentary dream wherein he is being pursued across a moonlit landscape by baying hounds.
6499. An ossified venom gland of a large spider that are prized as rare gems when set in Draenic jewelry.
6500. An oval-shaped soapstone tablet, inscribed with a short list of religious prohibitions
6501. An oval-shaped stone the size of a human eye, made from the darkest obsidian. The color is the purest black, and the glossy surface shines like a mirror. The reflections are strangely distorted, as it seems to reflect shadows rather than light. It is rumored in some occult circles that in the same way that a person can fall sick from a dark plague, so too can a ray of light become infected by shadow.
6502. An owl feather quill that forces the bearer to talk in the third person.
6503. An owlbear mask made from an actual preserved owlbear face.
6504. An ten feet of rope fashioned from straw-coloured strands of hair.
6505. An ugly, oddly-shaped gem that thrums with residual magical energy.
6506. An umbilical cord in a neat wooden box, lined with velvet. The cord itself has small, glassy eyes over its surface.
6507. An unassuming steel flask with the name “Tim” etched into its bottom.
6508. An unblemished black marble that reflects no light and can never be marked or scuffed
6509. An unbreakable fist sized, clear crystal sphere filled with swirling green smoke
6510. An unbreakable glass sphere containing a living ooze in magical stasis.
6511. An uncannily familiar face etched into a piece of dead wood.
6512. An uncomfortable mask consisting of a black cloth hood, padded leather earmuffs, and shuttered opaque goggles that block vision. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as a hoodwink cowl which is typically used for blinded and deafening kidnapping victims or transporting creatures to secret locations.
6513. An uncommonly convincing glass eye that’s completely unusable due to being fully spherical.
6514. An uncut black gemstone. Occasionally it makes the sound of a hammer striking an anvil.
6515. An undelivered letter addressed to The Lord of the East. It simply states “kill you” repeatedly in small cramped handwriting for three pages, front and back.
6516. An unfinished, highly complex nautical knot made with a length of ship’s line.
6517. An unidentifiable red gemstone three inches long
6518. An unnecessarily sharp letter opener, filed to a very fine point. The wooden handle sports a beautiful carving of a mermaid resting peacefully under a choppy sea.
6519. An unremarkable gray stone covered in myriad tiny pink growths, ranging in shape from rough textured flowers to near-perfect spheres. Knowledgeable PC’s can identify the mineral as rhodochrosite.
6520. An unremarkable silver coin whose faces sport two expressions. One face is joyfully exuberant, and the other is depressingly dour.
6521. An unsettling ceramic figurine of a whale with a mouth full of victims.
6522. An unsigned note requesting a meeting at a local landmark. The date for the rendezvous has not yet arrived.
6523. An unused calligraphy kit
6524. An unused, stainless steel machete
6525. An unusual standing lamp made of brass sporting a vented wheel over top of its wick and a number of crystal chimes along its outer edges. When lit, the rising hot air from the flame slowly turns the wheel which has a number of outward reaching pins which strike the chimes creating soothing tinkling noises, while the light refracting from the crystals creates a rosy glow. While much more suited for an upscale pleasure den, the lamp is sturdy enough for travel if carefully wrapped in fabric beforehand. The lamp will burn for six hours on a flask (One pint) of oil.
6526. An urgent letter requesting help. The date indicates that it’s from over a hundred years ago, but its linguistics are more suited to more current times.
6527. Apple of Doubt: A fruit that looks like an apple, it tastes like an apple, it smells like an apple, for all intents and purposes it appears to be an apple, but you are certain it is not an apple.
6528. Automatic Whetstone: A small unremarkable whetstone that never wears out and when touched to a dull edge and let go, starts sharpening it automatically. The whetstone stops and falls to the ground when the edge has been sharpened to perfection.
6529. Bag of Useless Junk. A tattered burlap sack that jingles as if it were full of broken glass, rusty nails and wooden scraps even when empty. The bag actually generates its own trash and three times per day, the bearer can reach into the sack and withdraw a Random Worthless Trinket.
6530. Bane of the Kitchen’s Apprentice: A white sous chef hat that causes the bearer to cause any recipe to go awry, no matter their skill or the simplicity of the recipe. The food will come out tasting ghastly anytime the hat is worn while the bearer is cooking.
6531. Barbed Vest: A black leather dotted with hundreds of tiny, barbed, fishhook-like needles, which are covered in thin leather flaps that prevent harm to the bearer. The barbs function as a last line of defense against close quarter attacks. A creature that injures the bearer with a natural weapon (Claws, teeth, fist, tail swipe) or successfully grapples the bearer has a 50%!c(MISSING)hance of taking 1 hitpoint’s worth of damage as they catch themselves on the barbs. Any creature that swallows the bearer takes 1 point of damage each round until it either spits him up, the bearer escapes, or the bearer dies (At which point the vest has sustained enough damage to no longer serve as a threat). The vest can be worn over simple clothes as well as lighter armors such as padded or studded leather but not over anything heavier.
6532. Beauticians clippers: Once per day the bearer can use the clippers to cause finger or toenails grow as if a month had gone by allowing damaged nails to be trimmed and cleaned much easier.
6533. Coin of Indecision: A gold coin with the word “YES” on one side and the word “NO” on the other. If it is flipped while asking a question, the coin always lands on edge.
6534. Darkvision Spectacles, Damaged: A pair spectacles made of smoked glass lenses set in a wire frame that fits most humanoids. Knowledgeable PC’s recognize these as type of magical item known as Darkvision Spectacles. Normally these glasses would allow a creature to see as if it had Darkvision, allowing the user to see in black and white out to the limits of their vision while in perfect darkness. It seems as though the enchantment is failing, the item was damaged, or the item simply wasn’t created properly, as the darkvision no longer functions. The remaining magic seems to be trying to overcompensate and creatures looking through the spectacles only see in black-and-white and are not able to discern color.
6535. Eye of Tyr: A gift from the God of Justice, this pommel gem gleams with a holy fire. Legends say that if the stone were to be set into the hilt of a weapon of virtue by a true believer and wielded by a warrior of the faith, the result would be a blade capable of carving a righteous swath through all the evils that plague the Realms.
6536. Five crossbow bolts that have religious blessings etched onto the hafts.
6537. Five decorated armbands that label the bearer as a member of the Cult of Unseen Turmoil.
6538. Genevieve’s: A small tin canister containing a few dozen candies that (According to the labelling), were accidentally synthesized by the gnomish alchemist Genevieve Boghopper. They’re big lumpy balls that rapidly change colors, each representing a different flavor, until they come into contact with saliva. The instructions mention that trying to put it in your mouth at the right moment to get your favorite flavor is a challenge and fun game.
6539. Half of a broken mask of brittle iron. An impassive, large-eyed face shows on the front, genderless. Within, the mask is not smooth, but convoluted by the canals, chambers, and processes of the facial skeleton, as if this shattered mask once composed the front portion of a living skull.
6540. Half of a floorplan for a temple, castle, or some other structure
6541. Half of a snapped oak battle standard. “We will fight to the last” is written in dried blood on one side.
6542. Half of a templar’s amulet depicting the sorcerer king Hamanu
6543. heavy copper coin on which the face on the coin has been carved into an elegant skull. Knowledgeable PC’s will be able to recognize the mark as the symbol of a local thieves guild and can find a saying in thieves cant carved around the edge of the coin.
6544. Innocent Lockpicks: A set of nondescript masterwork thieves’ tools coated in a matte black lacquer, that seems brittle enough to break. They are favored by spies and thieves that cannot afford to be caught with the tools of their trade. As an action equivalent to drawing a weapon, the bearer can break the brittle coating of the tools, causing them to dissolve into a puff of smoke, destroying the evidence of any wrongdoing. Because of their fragile nature these tools have a chance of breaking. If the bearer rolls a natural 1 while using the tools, there is a 25%!c(MISSING)hance that the tools snaps and dissolves in his hands. The item’s properties are wholly mundane and do not register as magical.
6545. Kolain Drop: A small tin canister containing a few dozen candies made from amber sugar, spun into a shape resembling the outline of a teardrop (Although a cynic might claim they look like candy nooses) and coated in dark chocolate. Licking a Kolain makes it harder to concentrate on sad memories for a short while, but finishing a whole drop while focusing on a specific memory accentuates the positive emotions of that memory and makes it easier to deal with the associated negative emotions.
6546. Magebane Mine, Inert: A ordinary looking red brick that knowledgeable PC’s recognize as an ancient mage slaying mine. The theory was that it was able to detect the innate magical presence within an individual and detonate. It was an complex experimental weapon back in its day and doesn’t seem to work properly at the moment. The device has magical as well as mechanical components and they would be dotted randomly throughout entire towns in walls, roads and residential buildings effectively “protecting” whole areas from mages and spellcasting creatures. The mechanical components would explode if their enchantments were dispelled and their magical payload would detonate if their physical components were disabled, making them all but impossible to neutralize. In fact the most common way to disable one (Assuming it could be distinguished, as it blended in like a brick in a brick wall) was to purposely trigger it from afar which tended to cause massive property damage. A spellcaster was required for most of the mine’s construction which would explain why they were not mass produced. No mage of the time wanted to be responsible for massacring their own kind. This specific mine seems to be defective as it’s physical components are in disrepair and the enchantments have faded, but a skilled enough mage, engineer or artificer might be able to repair it.
6547. Mirror of Self-Interest: A small steel mirror that doesn’t reflect anything but the face of the person holding it. However, this image of the person is perfect, devoid of any flaws or incongruities with the holder’s appearance as though they were the most attractive person in the province. This image is also only visible to the bearer.
6548. Neverspill Mug: Any drink poured into this mug can never be spilled accidentally. Someone can be struck upside the head with it, without a single drop escaping.
6549. One square foot of preserved human skin showing a map that changes randomly.
6550. Orb of forgetfulness. When touched the orb you will remember the last thing you tried to think about but forgot. You will however forget why you wanted to remember it.
6551. Pocket Watch of The Far Realm: A blued steel pocket watch with a silver chain that always tells the accurate time of the entire plane of the far realm. The far realm is a place beyond space and time. The pocket watches hands move fast and sporadically, sometimes even gaining a third and fourth hand. It is completely useless at telling the time on the material plane.
6552. Reveler’s mug: A large horn mug that cannot be turned upside down while containing liquid. If one attempts to do so, the mug changes shape in their hands so that it does not spill.
6553. Scabbard of Readiness: A scabbard comprised of metal and leather straps that can be adjusted to fit most weapons. When the bearer is surprised by foes, this scabbard instantly teleports into its bearer hand. Any weapon inside the scabbard remains where it was and typically falls to the ground immediately.
6554. Seastone Chalice: A two-handled drinking cup is wrought from some sort of pale blue-green material and inlaid with gold. The outside is rimmed with salt crystals which regenerate when brushed away, but these don’t seem to affect the chalice itself. When water is placed into the cup, it is instantly rendered salty and undrinkable. In addition, the cup produces deafening sounds of surf until it is emptied. Non-water beverages, such as alcohol, milk, juice, etc. are unaffected.
6555. Several dried moose ears sewn together and fashioned into a sheath for an average sized dagger
6556. Shivered Timber: A large shard of wood from a pirate ship’s mast that is constantly tipped with frost. The implement can be used a focus for casting spells that deal cold damage.
6557. Song Collar: A set of iron tubes designed to aid traveling musicians in learning new or complex compositions for the lute, violin, or another similar stringed instrument. Built to fit snugly over a normal quarterstaff, this item consists of a long metal collar that slips down over the top half of the staff, roughly two feet long for a typical staff. The metal’s surface is covered with rows of small, dimpled indentations, ostensibly to provide a better grip on the staff. In reality, these indentations correspond with the fingering for a piece of music to be played on a preferred instrument of the owner. Song collars crafted gnome engineers as training tools for journeymen, enabling them to practice chords and notes while traveling without attracting attention. A creature can actively learn or practice an instrument while traveling and maintaining a normal walking pace. While practicing on the move, the bearer suffers disadvantage on perception checks and is only considered to be practicing half as quickly as normal. For example a PC attempting to learn ho to play the lute who trains while walking for eight hours is only considered to have practiced for four hours towards becoming proficient in the instrument.
6558. Sprigs of dried hemlock, mistletoe and nightshade bound together with a red ribbon.
6559. Stone Sleeve: A stone sleeve is little more than a narrow cloth tube that is tied to the inner forearm so the opening rests in the palm. The sleeve can then be filled with up to six good-sized throwing stones or sling bullets. The sleeve can be opened with ease, allowing the bearer to arm himself with one of the rocks stored within as a free action.
6560. The “alchemical” recipe and blueprint for a “Big Mama”, a strange series of nested barrels filled with gunpowder and nails and designed to detonate from a fuse.
6561. The blade of an ancient sword. A mysterious coat of arms is carved into it.
6562. The bleached white skull of a human child. A perceptive PC will notice that the interior of the top of the skull bears a small white lump. The lump is a gold key that had been glued inside the skull and covered in white paint.
6563. The broken horn of an minotaur, strung on a leather cord.
6564. The broken horn of an ogre mage, strung on a leather cord.
6565. The deed for a parcel of land in a realm unknown to you
6566. The deed to a crumbling old manor house.
6567. The deed to a mysterious abandoned house in the Dreamers’ Quarter, wrapped around the brass key to the front door.
6568. The fang of a white wyrm engraved with the name of a lost tribal chief.
6569. The gold-coloured fleece of an unknown species of mountain animal.
6570. The Heart of the Damned: An ancient dried human heart that still beats loud and strong and that takes its name from the legend of the man that it came from. Legend has it that during a brutal war, the cause of which has long been forgotten, a keep was besieged. The lord of the keep knew that his walls would soon be overrun and, rather than surrendering and having his people enslaved, he slew them all. When the besiegers next attacked they found the lord standing amongst the slain, the sole survivor of the entire keep. The lord was burned at the stake and the legends say that his heart would not burn not matter how hot the flames became. According to superstition, the lord’s tortured soul is bound to the heart and he will suffer the heat of the flames eternally until the curse is broken. The heart resists all damage and cannot be burned or destroyed. A proper series of blessings or rituals could cleanse the heart and lift the curse, if a being was so inclined to grant that sort of absolution to a mass murderer.
6571. The hilt of a dagger that was used to assassinate a king. Its onxy pommel glows ominously on nights with a full moon.
6572. The mostly straight bones of a humanoid bound with rough twine to make a macabre sort of ladder, rolled into a bundle.
6573. The pickled tentacle of a mind flayer.
6574. The Pipe Pipe: A smoking pipe that doubles as a musical pipe. If the bearer smokes and plays the instrument at the same time, he can magically control the shape of the smoke, creating small billowing forms.
6575. The preserved fanged skull of a vampire, any blood spilt on the skull is absorbed into it.
6576. The preserved finger of a hill giant
6577. The preserved head of a mummy.
6578. The preserved skull of a raven.
6579. The severed claw of some monstrous scaled creature, preserved and formed into a camping stool.
6580. The shell of an egg painted with scenes of human misery in disturbing detail
6581. The silver badge of a powerful and secretive organization, with writing etched on the back that defames that group.
6582. The skeletal hand with six fingers and a thumb. It a slight but constant aroma of brimstone.
6583. The skeleton of an unknown animal encased in a mound of yellowing resin. It is much like a garden frog in both size and shape, albeit a garden frog with long claws and a protracted, toothy snout.
6584. The skull of a dire tiger, painted in infernal runes and mounted upside-down on a pedestal of black basalt carved in the shape of an erinyes who holds the basin up. The skull’s eye sockets are filled with blood coloured star-sapphires.
6585. The steel tip of a horn capped in plain steel and pointed like the punch-shape of a bodkin arrow. Dry, keratinous splinters of horn, fragile with age, are still stuck on the inside.
6586. The stuffed and preserved remains of a large bat.
6587. Three knuckle-bones that have been carved into dice.
6588. Three small crystal vials of what appear to be red blood. The vials are marked with druidic signs for son, beast-man and bird.
6589. Three stones linked together by a sturdy piece of rope, the stones are engraved with the words for ‘beginning’, ‘middle’ and ‘end.’
6590. Two coin sized disks of magnetized steel with layer of iron filaments sandwiched between them. If the disks are separated by a few inches, a miniature tornado comprised of iron filaments springs up between them anchored on each end to a disk.
6591. Two glass eyeballs in a tarnished silver box with gold hinges. The orbs lie on dusty, red velvet divots. They are green glass and irregularly shaped in the back. Oddly convoluted inside, like jelly and fish eggs. Gold leaf irises lie under the hard, crystal lenses.
6592. Two toy soldiers, one with a missing head
6593. Unending Chalk: A stick of chalk can be used to draw, but never gets shorter or breaks.
6594. Wand of False Life: An elm wand that if set down on a solid surface, will sprout tiny legs and arms, and move around like a living thing, spontaneously wandering around in a small area and sometimes dancing, particularly if there is music being played. It isn’t actually alive and will not move more than three feet from where it was placed. The arms and legs will fold away if the wand is picked up, but it always seems to have an elongated face as part of the grain of the wood at one end.
6595. Wrapped in a gold handkerchief is a red bamboo finger puzzle patterned with white stars. Suspended in the middle of the puzzle is a small, fluffy feather.
6596. A broken harlequin mask.
6597. A chess piece fashioned to look like fire myrmidon.
6598. A complex, but delicately made, marble maze puzzle.
6599. A conch shell which is always damp and constantly drips saltwater.
6600. A convincing replica of a large ruby, made entirely of crystallized sugar.
6601. A copper piece with a bearded king that no one recognizes on one side and a columned temple on the other.
6602. A copper spoon that tastes of raw meat.
6603. A corked empty vial that is constantly frosted with ice on the outside.
6604. A crown fashioned from vines.
6605. A crude chalice made of coal.
6606. A crumpled piece of parchment with an inked grid. Can be written on and then erased if crumpled again. 1974 charges remain.
6607. A crystal containing a trapped stirge. Emits a faint glow at night.
6608. A crystal cube with light trapped inside. Squeeze to illuminate a room for 1 round a day.
6609. A crystal with a face that bears uncanny likeness to you etched inside of it
6610. A deck of dirty playing cards. Each illustration depicts a different humanoid race, even some beastly ones
6611. A deep blue piece of flint, that when struck with steel produces not a spark but a drop of water.
6612. A dog-eared map, folded several times over. While it does not portray your current location, the landmarks seem familiar.
6613. A doll with hair made of rare moss.
6614. A dried out chasme honey globule, the smell is still pungent.
6615. A drinking cup, that randomly fills with fresh or salt water. Refilling once emptied.
6616. A dull spoon engraved with the name Heartseeker.
6617. A faceless doll made of driftwood.
6618. A failed adventurer's suicide note. I should have died down there with the others…
6619. A fashionable domino mask.
6620. A few feet of rope made from spider silk.
6621. A finely preserved sheep's bladder.
6622. A fist sized metallic pyramid, deeply etched in incomprehensible runes.
6623. A fist sized rock that beats like a heart.
6624. A fist-sized cog, covered in barnacles.
6625. A fist-sized lump of moss that seems to be breathing.
6626. A flag for a country which no longer exists.
6627. A flask of blue liquid which smells faintly of urine.
6628. A flask which has had its inside surface blackened by soot.
6629. A flat stone with white chalk marks that change each time it is observed.
6630. A frog statuette from which the legs have been broken off.
6631. A frost-covered silver locket that's frozen shut.
6632. A fur-lined cloak that looks like a pile of leaves.
6633. A furry, feathery pouch made from the hide of an owlbear
6634. A game board and pieces for a game no one knows how to play.
6635. A genie's veil.
6636. A gilded egg, about the size of your fist. If you listen carefully something can be heard scratching and muttering to itself inside it
6637. A glass bottle of water containing a live Quipper that is too large for the mouth of the container.
6638. A glass bottle with a tiny ship of unfamiliar design inside.
6639. A glass elf ear.
6640. A glass jar containing the preserved corpse of an unfamiliar aquatic creature.
6641. A glass lens set in a cheap metal rim. The glass is scuffed, but it makes reading slightly easier on weak eyes.
6642. A glass pendant with a hole in the center that a mild breeze always blows out of.
6643. A gnome's traveling journal. All pages are taken up by lewd drawings.
6644. A gold finger band that has three small glimmering stones set within; two glow with a faint reddish light while the third glows with a pale white luminosity.
6645. A gold hexagonal prism filled with dead but moving ants.
6646. A gold ring. Looking through it grants True Sight to 30 feet, but the field of view is far too narrow to be used in combat
6647. A golden amulet in the shape of a bug on a leaf.
6648. A golden bracelet, engraved with elvish words commemorating the unity of two great elven houses.
6649. A gourd with dried herbs. Whispers drift through the air when it is shaken
6650. A halfling's traveling journal. Most pages unused, what little is written makes fun of people's hats and noses.
6651. A hand mirror that reflects an unknown eye at all times.
6652. A hand-broom enchanted to constantly sweep up whatever room it’s placed in.
6653. A heavy iron key bearing the name of a ship long lost to the sea.
6654. A hemp necklace that amplifies the wearer’s voice slightly.
6655. A horse shoe which makes a slight humming sound at all times.
6656. A jar of gooey salve with small eyeballs in it. It heals magical blindness over the course of ten minutes.
6657. A jar of ink that seems like perfectly normal ink, but when used, it takes a 12 hours to appear.
6658. A jar of live bees.
6659. A jar of oil with a troll finger floating inside.
6660. A journal filled with poetry hand-written in Primordial.
6661. A knife enchanted so that the dull edge appears sharp and vise versa
6662. A kobold tooth with a glowing rune etched into it.
6663. A lace garter in black and red.
6664. A lady's coin purse containing two sharp fangs.
6665. A large brass coin with no markings or images on it.
6666. A large tooth with a channel running through. Now dry, it once came from a creature that used venom to paralyze or poison
6667. A lead model of a dragon, about three inches across.
6668. A leather eyepatch set with obsidian and a braided leather cord.
6669. A leather pouch full of rainbow colored sand.
6670. A leather pouch labeled ‘interesting rocks’.
6671. A leather-bound book full of pages made of dried leaves.
6672. A leather-cover, bound book of 34 handwritten pages. It contains the descriptions of a naturalist travelling through the Dessarin Valley
6673. A letter from a relative addressing you by a different name
6674. A lock that opens when blood is dripped in the keyhole
6675. A locket containing a picture of an ogre and a manticore.
6676. A long narrow box of stretched and hardened leather, this scroll case is surprisingly rigid. Investigation reveals, the leather is stretched over a framework of thin, but strong, copper wire.
6677. A long scroll case of bleached bone, that was once the leg bone of some tall creature. Stoppered at one end with a bone plug a leather cap fits over the other.
6678. A lump of coal that appears to be burning but sheds no heat and is never consumed
6679. A magical signet-ring that leaves a seal in wax that only the person or people you designate can see
6680. A manuscript of a dwarven poem about a couple who debate whether to go against the will of their clans for love. Ultimately they decide not to.
6681. A map of a forested area indicating the locations of various wolf dens.
6682. A marriage certificate between a Duke of Blackmail and a Baroness of Kidnappers.
6683. A merchant's scale you can adjust with a handwave to weigh slightly in your favor
6684. A metal tube about 6 inches long. When you press a button, a narrowly-focused beam of colored light shoots out from it
6685. A miniature brass horn, silent when played, but fills the air with the scent of warm and exotic spices.
6686. A mirror that only shows the back of your head.
6687. A moderately well crafted glass eye. It turns black on nights of a full moon.
6688. A monkey's tail.
6689. A monocle. Looking through it inverts the colors of what you see
6690. A music box that appears to be in working order but makes no noise.
6691. A necklace made of the desiccated eyes of numerous different creatures, no two of which are alike.
6692. A necklace made with githyanki fingerbones
6693. A necklace of varyingly spherical and odd-shaped shimmering stones that gives the wearer prophetic dreams
6694. A necklace with a small, rusted iron anchor.
6695. A needle made of gold that can pierce any hide.
6696. A nose flute.
6697. A note scrawled in blood. The symbols and shapes are indecipherable
6698. A novice wizard's booklet intended as an rudimentary introduction to performing the somatic component of spells.
6699. A pair of black stone dice that always roll a one and a six, respectively.
6700. A pair of bronze scissors in the shape of a pair of leaping dolphins.
6701. A pair of excruciatingly detailed false glass eyes, in grey and green. While held or placed on a solid surface, they gradually turn to face each other.
6702. A pair of green leather archery bracers. They have a pouch on the wrist for extra bowstrings
6703. A pair of magically linked bits of gemstone, holding one lets you sense the direction of the other
6704. A pair of obnoxious dragon tooth cufflinks. Wearer compelled to act haughty in public.
6705. A pair of strung together monocles. One is tinted red, the other green
6706. A pair of tetrahedral clay dice that always add up to five when rolled together
6707. A palm-sized stone with a hole in it, through which can be heard a constantly whispering wind.
6708. A paper fan that won't produce a breeze no matter how hard it's waved.
6709. A parchment flip book showing a knight galloping on a horse.
6710. A perfectly cubes blue-and-black-marbled gem
6711. A perfectly round black obsidian orb 2 in diameter with a small wedge-shaped chunk taken out of it
6712. A perfectly smooth stone which, when pressed, turns blue for 5 minutes.
6713. A picture amulet showing an opalescent blue altar. Provides lightning resistance 1/day while underground.
6714. A piece of petrified wood carved into the shape of a seashell.
6715. A pinch of lich dust in a tiny batskin pouch.
6716. A pinecone painted with glitter and gold on a small loop of twine.
6717. A plain looking, but finely forged ring with a single setting holding an overly large crystal . The crystal glows with faint red, blue and yellow hues.
6718. A plaque commemorating the death of an elven hero.
6719. A pocket-watch that started running backwards the moment you picked it up
6720. A preserved eyeball attached to a spindly eye-stalk, the nerves twitch occasionally

These tokens are typically given out by stage performers prior to their presentations as a keepsake or used as a ticket of admission to closed-door performances.

7054. A glass orb filled with randomly colored smoke.

7057. A gruesome drinking vessel that could be more accurately described as an unholy abomination. This small wineskin is entirely made of the soft flayed skin of a human baby. The pink flesh is pristine and unmarked, with the hideous exception of the partially healed burn of a branding iron that left the profane symbol of the Archdemon of god of evil domains. The infant’s eyes, ears and nose have been expertly sewn shut but its mouth has been converted to serve as the vessel’s spout. A palpable aura of defilement, corruption and sheer unrepentant evil emanates from the skin pouch and any liquid drank from it tastes of warm fresh blood that stains the mouth and soul. Any creature who views the wretched object and who possesses even the faintest shred of morality inside of them is overcome with the urge to douse the monstrosity in holy oil and set it ablaze.
7058. A hand sized origami paper crane in randomly colored that will glide ten feet when thrown and always lands gracefully.
7059. A holy symbol of a God of good domains made stained glass and wire, strung on a bright a brightly colored ribbon. If hung in a window it catches the light and reflects it in colourful patterns around the room.
7060. A holy symbol of a God of good domains, carved from a single chunk of soapstone. Its graceful curves draw the eye and sooth the mind.
7061. A holy symbol of a random god long thought dead and gone. Although made only of iron, it resists any non magical attempts to damage it. Knowledgeable PC’s can determine that the reason for its durability is due to the faintest whisper of divine power still residing within it.
7062. A holy symbol of a minor God of god of evil domains that hangs from a particularly long chain that allows it to be worn at navel height. Some of the chain’s links are particularly worn.
7063. A holy symbol of a minor God of lawful domains that has a secret compartment; it contains scraps of bone and hair, remnants of a great hero of the faith. It is believed the remains imbued the symbol with extra power.
7064. A holy symbol that depicts symbols and images meaningful to a forbidden cult dedicated to a demon lord of chaotic domains.
7065. A large belt pouch containing a small book wrapped in cloth. The book has a battered, mildewed cover and faded writing. Close examination reveals it is a prayer book of the minor god of god of evil domains.
7066. A large hourglass with a sturdy wooden frame and thick glass obviously meant for rough travel and adventuring. The hourglass contains randomly colored sand which takes 24 hours to pass from top to bottom when flipped.
7067. A large, randomly colored, wooden box decorated with musical notes. It contains a full set of unused piano strings made of steel wire.
7068. A laurel of bright a brightly colored flowers which never wilt or die.
7069. A long priest’s stole made of a fine silken fabric which has various divine symbols belonging to a random god sewn upon it vertically down each side of the stole.
7070. A mask made of randomly colored coral that wraps around the bearer’s face.
7071. A metal holy symbol of a minor God of god of evil domains, comprised of the melted down remains of several holy symbols of minor gods of assorted non-evil domains. A perceptive PC can make out several different good-aligned defaced symbols on its surface.
7072. A normal looking bronze ingot that upon close inspection reveal, a seam down its center. With a modicum of force it will split into two halves, revealing the inverse reliefs of each side of the holy symbol of the random god.

7074. A pair of heavy chain manacles carved from randomly colored jade.
7075. A pair of leather and glass goggles whose lenses are tinted randomly colored.
7076. A pair of leather and glass goggles whose lenses glow a faint a brightly colored . The bearer is not negatively affected by the glow and can see normally

7078. A pair of wire and glass spectacles whose lenses are tinted randomly colored.
7079. A pair of wire and glass spectacles whose lenses glow a faint a brightly colored. The bearer is not negatively affected by the glow and can see normally.
7080. A playing card shaped, randomly colored, wooden token with unique symbols on it. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as a calling card of a local medium. These tokens are typically given out by mediums prior to their presentations as a keepsake or used as a ticket of admission to closed-door seances.
7081. A porcelain disk painted with a detailed representation of a random god .
7082. A randomly colored metal sphere, one-inch in diameter that is always warm to the touch
7083. A randomly colored ceramic disc with a single jagged rune violently carved into its surface.
7084. A randomly colored felt hat decorated with dozens of feathers, each from a different species of bird.
7085. A randomly colored handkerchief with a knot in it, the owner probably had something important they didn’t want to forget.
7086. A randomly colored marble that hovers three inches above whatever surface it’s placed on.
7087. A randomly colored silk scarf that purrs audibly when stroked
7088. A randomly colored silk scarf that says “Gods bless you.” whenever someone sneezes within earshot of it.
7089. A randomly colored triangular flag emblazoned with strange markings which, when waved, plays an unfamiliar tune.
7090. A randomly colored, wooden box decorated with musical notes. It contains a full set of unused strings made of sheep gut sized for a large standing harp.
7091. A random musical instrument that sounds slightly different to everyone that hears it.
7092. A round piece of high quality linen with a hole in the center for the head, which drapes to the waist, and forms a semicircle when the bearer’s arms are raised to the sides as seen from the front or back. Knowledgeable PC’s will recognize this as a priest’s chasuble used for the religious ceremonies of a random god and is coloured and decorated to match the deity or religion.
7093. A shimmering randomly colored crystal with the image of a dragon inside it.
7094. A simple randomly colored headband with a clear crystal set in the center of the brow.
7095. A single randomly colored dragon scale, worn away by time.
7096. A small bag containing a large brass coin stamped with the insignia of the archdemon of god of evil domains , along with a red cultist mask. There is also a map of the nearby area that indicates a meeting location somewhere in the distant woods. A perceptive PC will notice that the map reveals a passphrase “Bloodmoon” hidden within the drawing.

7098. A small brass statuette taken from the nearby shrine of a minor God of good domains

7123. A tarnished brass coin engraved with the holy symbol of a minor God of god of evil domains.

7126. A twelve-sided die crafted of solid randomly colored glass
7127. A type of novelty lamp that was popular among some social circles a few decades ago. It consists of a randomly colored wax mixture inside a conical glass vessel, the remainder of which contains a translucent liquid of a complimenting color. The vessel rests on a polished steel frame containing a candle whose heat and light are channeled into the bottom of the glass where the wax rests. The warmed wax rises through the surrounding liquid, cools, loses its buoyancy, and falls back to the bottom of the vessel in a cycle that is visually suggestive of lava, hence the commonly associated name of the lamp.
